# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V3.011

## The Green Climate Fund (GCF)

The "Green Climate" concept of the Great Green Wall (GGW) are across the least developed countries and the most vulnerable to climate change. The GGW is a pan-African initiative with the aim of restoring degraded landscapes in order to improve livelihoods, enhance food security and increase resilience to climate change. The GGW is a living wall that brings together people from different countries, cultures and backgrounds to work towards a common goal. It is a holistic approach that addresses the root causes of desertification and land degradation, such as unsustainable land use practices, drought, deforestation and overgrazing.

### Sub-national Resilience Alliance (SURAGGONIA)

SURAGGONIA is a USD 291,984,235 multi-country initiative led by Ethiopia. It aims to restore degraded landscapes in the Horn of Africa through sustainable land management practices and improved livelihoods. The SURAGGONIA project will focus on restoring degraded lands and promoting sustainable land use practices in the region.

#### Objectives

- Restore 10 million hectares of degraded land by 2030.
- Increase carbon sequestration by 50% by 2030.
- Improve the livelihoods of 1.5 million people by 2030.

#### Activities

The SURAGGONIA project will focus on restoring degraded lands and promoting sustainable land use practices in the region. The project will also aim to improve the livelihoods of local communities through the provision of improved technologies and access to markets. The project will be implemented in collaboration with local governments, civil society organizations and other stakeholders.

#### Implementation

The SURAGGONIA project will be implemented through a participatory approach that involves local communities, civil society organizations and other stakeholders. The project will also be aligned with the objectives of the Paris Agreement and the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs). The project will be implemented in phases, with each phase focusing on a specific area of the region.

#### Partnerships

The SURAGGONIA project will be implemented in partnership with local governments, civil society organizations and other stakeholders. The project will also be aligned with the objectives of the Paris Agreement and the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs). The project will be implemented in phases, with each phase focusing on a specific area of the region.

#### Sustainability

The SURAGGONIA project will aim to achieve sustainability by promoting sustainable land use practices and improving the livelihoods of local communities. The project will also aim to promote gender equality and empower women and girls. The project will be implemented in partnership with local governments, civil society organizations and other stakeholders.

#### Monitoring and Evaluation

The SURAGGONIA project will be monitored and evaluated through a comprehensive monitoring and evaluation system that includes regular reporting and periodic reviews. The project will also be subject to an independent evaluation at the end of the implementation period.

# B. PROJECT PROGRAMME INFORMATION

## B.1 Complete context images (900 words equivalent/2 pages)

### Climate change problems[^1]

1. The eight countries of the Great Green Wall (GGW) included in the SURAGGRA program are among the world's most vulnerable to climate change and desertification. These countries face significant challenges related to food security, income and employment, water scarcity, and natural resource degradation. Climate change exacerbates these issues by increasing temperatures, altering precipitation patterns, and causing more frequent and severe droughts. This has led to increased pressure on land and water resources, resulting in conflicts over scarce resources.

2. During the last 40 years, the mean temperature has risen by nearly 1°C in the Sahel region, with an increase of up to 3°C in some areas. This warming trend is expected to continue, leading to more frequent and severe droughts. The GGW countries are also experiencing increased variability in precipitation, which affects agricultural productivity and food security.

3. Climate change has exacerbated the vulnerability of communities dependent on agriculture and livestock. It has led to a decrease in crop yields and pasture quality, as well as an increase in pests and diseases. This has resulted in reduced food security and increased poverty levels.

4. The impact of climate change is further compounded by other factors such as population growth, urbanization, and deforestation. These factors put additional pressure on natural resources and exacerbate the effects of climate change.

5. During the last 10 years, the mean temperature has risen by nearly 1°C in the Sahel region, with an increase of up to 3°C in some areas. This warming trend is expected to continue, leading to more frequent and severe droughts. The GGW countries are also experiencing increased variability in precipitation, which affects agricultural productivity and food security.

6. Climate change has exacerbated the vulnerability of communities dependent on agriculture and livestock. It has led to a decrease in crop yields and pasture quality, as well as an increase in pests and diseases. This has resulted in reduced food security and increased poverty levels.

7. The impact of climate change is further compounded by other factors such as population growth, urbanization, and deforestation. These factors put additional pressure on natural resources and exacerbate the effects of climate change.

After the climate analysis and in particular to the proposition of the programme included in area 3, this will cover the following:

- **Area 1:** Climate change impacts on agriculture.
- **Area 2:** Climate change adaptation strategies for farmers.
- **Area 3:** Climate change mitigation efforts.

The programme aims to address the challenges posed by climate change and support sustainable development. It will focus on improving agricultural practices, enhancing food security, and promoting climate resilience.

## 3. May and maximum temperature and both expected increase in all countries in the BARBADOS under both the RCP4.5 and RCP8.5 scenarios, following the trend identified in historical data while more intense heat waves are expected to occur. The projected changes in precipitation patterns will lead to an increased frequency of droughts and floods in the region. This will have a significant impact on agriculture, water resources, and energy production. Additionally, sea-level rise is expected to increase the risk of coastal flooding and erosion, which could lead to the displacement of populations.

## 4. The above mentioned climate trends? need to aggregate the negative impacts of poor agro-climatic practices on soil health and fertility, leading to increased soil degradation and desertification caused by human management, during a period when the region is already experiencing significant land degradation. This will exacerbate the challenges related to food security and nutrition, as well as increase the vulnerability of ecosystems and biodiversity. The impacts of climate change on water resources and energy production will also be exacerbated, leading to increased water scarcity and energy insecurity.

4. The Earth is highly exposed to climate change, and extreme weather events. The Green Fund proposal focuses on the most vulnerable countries, which are highly exposed to natural disasters caused by extreme weather events. Investing in adaptation measures will help these countries reduce their vulnerability to climate change. The Green Fund will also focus on reducing greenhouse gas emissions with the highest share of the population affected by natural hazards related to climate change. This includes projects that aim to reduce emissions from deforestation and forest degradation, as well as those that aim to increase carbon sequestration in forests.

Current Adaptation: In Flood Development Assistance Task, indicated that the probability of the rain during the rainy season is increasing. The total amount of rainfall has increased by 10 percent (from 200 mm/year) to 300 mm/year over the last 5 years. The total amount of rainfall in the dry season has also increased by 10 percent, from 40 mm/year to 60 mm/year over the same period. This increase in rainfall has led to an increase in flooding and landslides, which have caused significant damage to infrastructure and property.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.3/12

## B

The majority of the Sahel is mostly flat. Altitudes rarely range between 300 and 400 m above sea level, while most of the region is below 200 m. The Sahara Desert covers about 85% of the area, with only 15% remaining as semi-arid land. The Sahel has a semi-arid climate with distinct dry and wet seasons. The rainy season lasts from May to October, receiving from barely 100 mm to over 600 mm of rainfall annually. The region is characterized by its natural resources such as forests, having abundant water resources, while others such as Chad have minimal resources.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.31.5 - PAGE 1

## 1.E. The Saharan Oasis Ecosystem

The Saharan Oasis ecosystem comprises of farms, settlements, villages, grazing lands and pastures which are surrounded by deserts. These oases have been the source of livelihood for humans since ancient times. They provide food, water, fodder and other essential products to the local communities. The oases also act as a corridor for migratory species that help sustain human health and enhance biodiversity. However, these ecosystems face numerous challenges such as climate change, overgrazing, deforestation, pollution, and overexploitation of resources. These issues have led to degradation of the ecosystem services provided by the oases.

### Key Benefits and Constraints

- **Conservations Funded by Sustainable Farms and Herders**: Livelihoods in the Saharan oasis heavily depend on sustainable farming practices. The conservation fund is a vital source of income for local communities. It helps to maintain the balance between development and conservation.

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a new funding mechanism established under the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC). The GCF aims to support projects and programmes in developing countries that will contribute to mitigation and adaptation to climate change. The GCF was created to serve as a mechanism for mobilizing financial resources from developed countries to assist developing countries in achieving their climate goals.

## Main Barriers

- Lack of knowledge on local community needs
- Rural communities have limited access to information and technology
- Women lack input knowledge, decision-making power and control over land
- Poor maintenance conditions and labor practices for women's work
- Lack of adequate technical facilities and financial resources
- Limited access to credit and other financial services

## Challenges Facing Women

- Lack of family planning facilities and services
- Lack of access to education and training opportunities
- Lack of access to health care and social security benefits
- Lack of access to land, housing and property rights
- Lack of access to credit and other financial services
- Limited participation in decision-making processes
- Lack of adequate technical facilities and financial resources

## Mitigating Factors

- Lack of family planning facilities and services
- Lack of access to education and training opportunities
- Lack of access to health care and social security benefits
- Lack of access to land, housing and property rights
- Lack of access to credit and other financial services
- Limited participation in decision-making processes
- Lack of adequate technical facilities and financial resources

### Enhancing the private and public governance with the GCF

- Strengthen the capacity of the GCF to effectively manage its resources.
- Task force on climate-related financial disclosures to include for the private sector.

### Weak institutional powers of the government to implement, enforce, fund or monitor environmental policies

- Lack of political will and commitment to environmental issues.
- Inadequate funding and resources allocated to environmental agencies.
- Weak enforcement of environmental laws and regulations.
- Limited capacity of environmental agencies to effectively manage and implement environmental policies.
- Lack of public awareness and education on environmental issues.

### Absence of the parties and their representatives in the decision-making process

- Lack of stakeholder engagement in policy formulation and implementation.
- Limited participation of local communities in environmental decision-making processes.
- Lack of transparency and accountability in environmental governance.

## 15. Constraints faced by the Private sector

The private sector has been facing numerous challenges that hinder their ability to effectively participate in climate change mitigation and adaptation efforts. Some of these challenges include:

- Lack of access to affordable financing options for green projects.
- High costs of technology and equipment required for green initiatives.
- Limited access to markets and customers for green products and services.
- Lack of regulatory frameworks and incentives that support private sector engagement in climate action.
- Lack of capacity building and technical assistance for the private sector.

## 16. Constraints faced by Public Sector

The public sector faces numerous challenges that hinder their ability to effectively implement climate change policies and programs. Some of these challenges include:

- Limited financial resources and budget constraints.
- Lack of political will and commitment to climate change action.
- Inadequate capacity and technical expertise in climate change mitigation and adaptation.
- Lack of coordination and collaboration between different government agencies and departments responsible for climate change.
- Lack of public awareness and engagement on climate change issues.

## 21. The selection of the current and city-wide initiatives could Boost Resilience in Africa's Green Climate Fund

The GCF can play a significant role in supporting these initiatives by providing funding, technical assistance, and capacity building to help them achieve their goals.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.311.5 - PAGE 17

## Types of activities meeting eligibility in relevant languages

More specifically, the programme seeks to address the needs of countries that are:

- **Non-Annex I Parties** and **non-EU Accession Countries**

### Key activities
- **Reduction of GHG emissions**
    - **Reforestation and Afforestation Activities (REA)**
        - **Reforestation**: Planting new forests on lands that have not been forested or afforested in the past 50 years.
        - **Afforestation**: Planting new forests on lands that have been deforested.

- **Enhancement of carbon stocks**
    - **Avoided Deforestation (AD)**
        - Activities to prevent deforestation and degradation of forests, such as sustainable forest management and conservation efforts.

### Eligibility criteria
- Projects must be additional, i.e., they should go beyond what would have happened anyway.
- Projects must be sustainable in the long term.
- Projects must be consistent with the objectives of the GCF.

## Benefits of the programme

The programme offers several benefits to countries and communities:

- **Economic benefits**: Increased revenue from carbon credits generated by successful projects.
- **Environmental benefits**: Reduced GHG emissions and enhanced carbon stocks.
- **Social benefits**: Improved livelihoods for local communities involved in project activities.

### Key components
- **Capacity building**: Training and support for local communities to implement and manage projects effectively.
- **Technology transfer**: Provision of appropriate technologies to enhance project outcomes.
- **Monitoring and evaluation**: Regular assessment of project progress and impact.

## Funding structure

The programme is funded through a combination of public and private sources, including:

- **Government contributions**
    - **National governments**: Provide core funding for the programme.
    - **International organizations**: Contribute to specific projects or activities.

- **Private sector investments**
    - **Corporate social responsibility (CSR) initiatives**: Companies invest in projects that align with their sustainability goals.
    - **Carbon trading**: Revenue generated from carbon credits is reinvested into the programme.

## Implementation

The programme is implemented through a network of regional and national implementing entities, which work closely with local communities to identify and develop projects. These entities provide technical assistance, capacity building, and monitoring support to ensure the successful implementation of projects.

### Key challenges
- **Financing**: Securing sufficient funding to support all eligible projects.
- **Capacity**: Building the capacity of local communities to implement and manage projects effectively.
- **Coordination**: Ensuring effective coordination among different stakeholders, including governments, private sector entities, and international organizations.

## Conclusion

The GCF programme offers a comprehensive approach to addressing climate change by promoting sustainable land use practices, enhancing carbon stocks, and supporting economic development. Through effective implementation and strong partnerships, the programme can achieve significant environmental and social benefits while contributing to global efforts to mitigate climate change.

## Knowledge and strong monitoring links

Changes in land use, especially commercial farms, and deforestation of natural forests are major drivers of biodiversity loss and climate change. SURAGGWA is based on the premise that successful initiatives can be scaled up through regional cooperation. The project will support the development of a regional network of biodiversity experts, including local communities, to monitor GEF results, with such improved coordination and communication. This will enhance the capacity of national governments to implement large-scale climate action treatments, as well as creating knowledge solutions for scaling up successful initiatives. The project will also provide technical assistance to countries through the Secretariat for higher impact and sustained implementation.

### Section 5: The large majority of the eight SURAGGWA countries, which highly diversify, do share a considerable number of important problems. Common challenges include deforestation, degradation, illegal logging, and poaching. These issues are exacerbated by the lack of coordination among countries in the region. ### Section 6: Cross-sectoral linkages, improved monitoring, regional networks, and knowledge sharing are essential for successful implementation of large-scale climate action treatments. ### 31. Stakeholder engagement strategy

Stakeholders' consent and engagement are some of the factors that ensure the inclusion, ownership, and sustainability of projects. This includes the identification of key stakeholders who will be affected by the project and their participation in the planning and implementation process. The project will engage with local communities to ensure that they have a say in the development and implementation of the project. The project will also engage with other stakeholders such as government agencies, non-governmental organizations, and private sector entities.

### 32. GURAGGMA's current activities

GURAGGMA is currently involved in several activities designed to help make rural livelihoods more resilient to climate change. These include:

- **Tree planting**: GURAGGMA has been involved in tree planting activities that have helped to restore degraded landscapes and improve soil fertility.
- **Water harvesting**: GURAGGMA has also been involved in water harvesting activities that have helped to increase the availability of water for irrigation and domestic use.

### 33. Proposed activities

The proposed activities are designed to build on the current activities of GURAGGMA and to expand their scope and impact. The proposed activities include:

- **Climate change adaptation**: GURAGGMA will continue to engage in climate change adaptation activities such as tree planting, water harvesting, and soil conservation.
- **Capacity building**: GURAGGMA will also engage in capacity building activities that will help to increase the knowledge and skills of local communities in climate change adaptation and mitigation.
- **Community engagement**: GURAGGMA will continue to engage with local communities to ensure that they have a say in the development and implementation of the project.

### 34. Monitoring and evaluation

The project will be monitored and evaluated using a variety of methods, including:

- **Baseline surveys**: Baseline surveys will be conducted to establish the baseline status of the project.
- **Regular monitoring**: Regular monitoring will be carried out to track progress towards the project objectives.
- **Endline evaluations**: Endline evaluations will be conducted to assess the impact of the project.

### 35. Financial management

The financial management of the project will be carried out in accordance with the guidelines set by the Green Climate Fund. This includes:

- **Budgeting**: A detailed budget will be prepared for the project.
- **Financial reporting**: Regular financial reports will be submitted to the Green Climate Fund.

### 36. Risk management

The project will include a risk management plan that identifies potential risks and outlines measures to mitigate them. The risk management plan will be regularly reviewed and updated as necessary.

### 37. Gender equality and social inclusion

The project will promote gender equality and social inclusion by ensuring that:

- **Women and men have equal access to project benefits**.
- **Vulnerable groups are included in the project**.

### 38. Safeguards

The project will comply with the safeguard policies of the Green Climate Fund, including:

- **Environmental and social impact assessments**: These will be conducted to ensure that the project does not have adverse environmental or social impacts.
- **Grievance mechanisms**: Mechanisms will be put in place to address any grievances that may arise during the implementation of the project.

### 39. Co-financing

The project will seek co-financing from other sources, including:

- **Government agencies**
- **Non-governmental organizations**
- **Private sector entities**

### 40. Project summary

The project is designed to help rural communities in [Country] adapt to the impacts of climate change and improve their livelihoods. The project will build on the current activities of GURAGGMA and expand their scope and impact.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.31.1 - PAGE 8

## B

Except for partnership and bridge providing critical support to a climate finance “matchmaker,” the value of our proposal lies in the development of a comprehensive, community-based approach to ecosystem restoration that can be scaled up and replicated by communities that have been excluded from access to climate finance.

### 34. Additional: The set of interventions across both restoration and ETP chain development comprise a holistic approach to addressing the root causes of deforestation and forest degradation. As such, they are mutually reinforcing. For example:

- **Restoration** efforts can help to reduce pressure on forests by providing alternative sources of income for local communities.
- **ETP** value chains can provide additional incentives for communities to protect their restored forests.

### 35. **SURADONA**

SURADONA uses a holistic approach for biodiversity selection that prioritizes communities most vulnerable to land degradation, those with limited access to markets, and those who have been excluded from decision-making processes. The program will engage with local governments, community groups, and private sector actors to ensure the sustainability of interventions. It will also provide training on sustainable practices and support the development of green value chains.

Furthermore, the program will engage communities, governments, and the private sector in the development of a comprehensive approach to ecosystem restoration that includes:

- **Restoration** efforts to protect and restore degraded lands.
- **ETP** value chain development to create economic opportunities for local communities.
- **Policy engagement** to support the adoption of policies that promote sustainable land use.

### 36. **SURADONA**

SURADONA uses a holistic approach for biodiversity selection that prioritizes communities most vulnerable to land degradation, those with limited access to markets, and those who have been excluded from decision-making processes. The program will engage with local governments, community groups, and private sector actors to ensure the sustainability of interventions. It will also provide training on sustainable practices and support the development of green value chains.

Furthermore, the program will engage communities, governments, and the private sector in the development of a comprehensive approach to ecosystem restoration that includes:

- **Restoration** efforts to protect and restore degraded lands.
- **ETP** value chain development to create economic opportunities for local communities.
- **Policy engagement** to support the adoption of policies that promote sustainable land use.

## Logframe

### Objective

- **Overall Goal:** To contribute to the achievement of sustainable development goals (SDGs) through climate change mitigation and adaptation.
- **Specific Objectives:**
  - Enhance climate resilience in vulnerable communities.
  - Promote low-emission and climate-resilient development.

### Indicators

- **Outputs**
  - Number of projects implemented.
  - Number of beneficiaries trained.
  - Area of land restored or conserved.

- **Outcomes**
  - Reduction in greenhouse gas emissions.
  - Increase in community resilience to climate change.
  - Improved access to climate finance.

## Results Framework

### Outputs

| Output | Description |
|--------|-------------|
| O1     | Implementation of projects. |
| O2     | Training programs conducted. |
| O3     | Land restoration and conservation activities completed. |

### Outcomes

| Outcome | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| O4      | Greenhouse gas emissions reduced. |
| O5      | Community resilience enhanced. |
| O6      | Access to climate finance improved. |

## Logframe

### Indicators

- **Outputs**
  - Number of projects implemented.
  - Number of beneficiaries trained.
  - Area of land restored or conserved.

- **Outcomes**
  - Reduction in greenhouse gas emissions.
  - Increase in community resilience to climate change.
  - Improved access to climate finance.

## Selection criteria for the beneficiaries

For component B, a number of criteria have been elaborated to guide the selection process. These include:

1. **Necessity and relevance:** The proposed activities should address real needs and be relevant to the local context.
2. **Environmental impact:** Projects should contribute significantly to climate change mitigation or adaptation.
3. **Sustainability:** Activities should be designed to have long-term benefits and be sustainable without ongoing support.

The criteria will ensure that the selected beneficiaries are those most in need, with projects that have a genuine positive environmental impact and are likely to continue successfully after initial funding ends.

## Technical and organizational criteria

- **Capacity:** The organization must demonstrate the capacity to implement the project effectively.
- **Experience:** Previous experience in similar projects is an advantage.
- **Resources:** Availability of necessary resources (human, financial, technical) to carry out the activities.

## B

### Produce a report on the potential for carbon trading in the region.

1. Identify and evaluate opportunities for carbon trading.
2. Assess the feasibility of implementing a carbon trading scheme.
3. Develop a business case for carbon trading.
4. Conduct a cost-benefit analysis to determine the economic viability.
5. Evaluate the legal and regulatory framework required to support carbon trading.
6. Identify potential partners and stakeholders who can assist in the implementation.

### Benefits:

- **Environmental**: Reduces greenhouse gas emissions.
- **Economic**: Generates revenue through carbon credits.
- **Social**: Encourages sustainable practices among businesses.

### Risks:

- **Environmental**: Potential for market manipulation or fraud.
- **Economic**: Volatility in carbon prices can affect revenue.
- **Legal**: Compliance with international and national regulations is required.

### Opportunities:

- Collaboration with international organizations.
- Leveraging existing environmental initiatives.
- Utilizing technology to monitor and verify emissions.

### Baseline

Component 1: Large-scale landscape restoration to increase climate change resilience and mitigation, for and through sustainable land management.

**Outcome 1.1:** Multiple and modified degraded lands restored under climate resilient land management practices.

**Outcome 1.2:** GHG emissions reduced, avoided or removed elsewhere.

**Output 1.1:** Local community groups organized, trained and knowledgeable in land restoration activities and methods.

38. In order to implement this outcome, a needs assessment will be carried out using a GCLF planning tool developed by the Green Climate Fund (GCF) to identify priority areas for restoration. The GCF planning tool will be used to identify priority areas for restoration based on criteria such as land degradation, soil quality, water availability and climate change vulnerability. The planning process will also include an assessment of local community needs and capacities, including their willingness to participate in the restoration activities. The planning process will involve a participatory approach that includes input from local communities, civil society organizations, government agencies and other stakeholders.

**Activity 1.1.1:** Identify potential sites for restoration and inform resource base for value chain development.

39. The purpose of this activity is to assist communities and local authorities to work together on developing specific activities in the priority areas identified by the GCF planning tool. This will involve a participatory approach that includes input from local communities, civil society organizations, government agencies and other stakeholders. The activity will also include an assessment of the resources available for restoration and the potential for value chain development.

**Activity 1.1.2:** Identify and mobilize communities to participate actively in the specific sites, ensuring their participation in the planning process.

40. The purpose of this activity is to assist communities and local authorities to work together on developing specific activities in the priority areas identified by the GCF planning tool. This will involve a participatory approach that includes input from local communities, civil society organizations, government agencies and other stakeholders. The activity will also include an assessment of the resources available for restoration and the potential for value chain development.

**Outcome 1.3:** Through 1.2, the component does not have making or cash transfers elements to the concerned local authorities.

40. The primary goal of this proposal is to promote natural resource management and enhance resilience at local levels through NTFPs value chain development sub-projects (1.2.1), supported by activities (1.1.1) also. Subprojects, among others, will include the establishment of community-based enterprises for sustainable use of NTFP resources and promotion of technical assistance, help the GCF newly-created national adaptation plans at the local level to enhance climate resilience and promote sustainable management of natural resources. Community-based enterprises (CBEs), including species (e.g., honey, ICM), are commonly used in many countries as a means of promoting sustainable use of natural resources. CBEs can be established through community organizations or be created sub-activity 1.2.4. The establishment of CBEs will help to enhance the development of gender and integrated approaches (sub-activity 1.1.5).

41. The primary goal for implementation of this grant proposal is based on need capacity used by local Land Tenure and Forestry Committees (LTFs) and community-based organizations (CBOs). These groups will be supported to implement the activities of the Great Green Wall Compact through the establishment of CBEs, which will help to enhance the resilience of communities. The implementation of this proposal will be based on the principles of gender equality and social inclusion.

## Activity 1.1.3: Train program's mission teams and community management organizations

42. The program will include a "Training of Trainers" approach in a series of training sessions for mission team members to enhance their capacity to train local communities and CBEs on sustainable use of natural resources. This activity will be implemented through the following sub-activities:

### Sub-activity 1.1.3.1: Training of trainers

43. National experts, including forestry and NGO facilitators and other Service Providers, will train teams in Forest, Pasture, School, and Community Forest Management (FPSM). This training will be conducted through the FFS model, based on a comprehensive approach to community-based natural resource management.

### Sub-activity 1.1.3.2: Name and develop systems of governance to ensure the sustainability of genuinely appropriate seeds

44. Good physiological quality control for propagation and propagation and quantity of seeds and seedlings of non-timber forest products (NTFPs) will be ensured through the establishment of a seed bank in the Great Green Wall countries. There is a need for an inventory of good seeds from native species to ensure the sustainability of NTFP value chains. A seed bank will help to provide quality seeds and seedlings for planting, which will enhance the resilience of communities to climate change. Communities will be trained on how to establish and manage the seed banks. It is essential that local well (genetically) known seeds will be set up to ensure the sustainability of species 2.0 and local adaptation. The establishment of a seed bank will help to develop local knowledge for planting and site selection and ensuring and caring for the environment.

Recent tests are planned using trays and Defra plots which vary by planting pattern system and moisture content. These will be used to determine the optimum planting density of up to 600 trees per hectare. This land preparation method shows better establishment rates than direct drilling, particularly in wetter areas where soil structure is poor. This method also reduces the risk of erosion. The use of trays can be combined with other techniques such as mulching and planting in strips to optimize planting and seeding. We will consider using these methods for future restoration projects.

## Component 2: Development of climate-resilient, low-emission rural forest product (NFPP) value chains benefiting vulnerable consumer households

### Component 2: Development of climate-resilient, low-emission rural forest product (NFPP) value chains benefiting vulnerable consumer households

This component will build on the benefits of the local agro-ecological communities and enhance NFPP operations, promote and realize the Green Deal Wall outcomes in the selected pilot countries. The component will support the development of sustainable and resilient NFPP value chains through the following activities:

1. **Supporting the development of sustainable and resilient NFPP value chains**
   - Enhance the capacity of local agro-ecological communities to develop and implement sustainable and resilient NFPP value chains.
   - Promote the adoption of climate-smart practices in NFPP operations.

2. **Promoting the integration of NFPP operations into local economies**
   - Support the development of market linkages between NFPP producers and local markets.
   - Facilitate the integration of NFPP operations into local economic systems.

3. **Enhancing the resilience of NFPP value chains to climate change**
   - Promote the adoption of climate-smart practices in NFPP operations.
   - Support the development of early warning systems for climate-related risks.

### Milestones and Deliverables

- **Milestone 1**: Development of a detailed, results-based approach (Deliverable 2.1)
- **Milestone 2**: Technical and organizational capacity assessment completed (Deliverable 2.2)
- **Milestone 3**: Enhanced access to financial services for NFPP operations (Deliverable 2.3)

### 63
Under component 2, the planned country level activities are to be supported by ten categories of analytical level activities. These include: (i) identification of potential investment opportunities; (ii) assessment of risks and challenges associated with financing; (iii) sharing of lessons learned from experience and partnerships; (iv) the promotion of the NIIF in the region; (v) capacity building for private sector engagement; (vi) project evaluation and monitoring; and (vii) knowledge management. These activities are to be implemented by the GCF Secretariat, the Green Climate Fund, the World Bank Group, the International Finance Corporation and other relevant organizations. The Green Climate Fund is to provide funding for these activities.

The policy, regulatory and enabling environment by business in the region is complex and not adequately tailored to support private sector engagement in climate change mitigation and adaptation projects. # GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.31.1 - PAGE 5

## B

### Unit Activity 2.1.1

The programme will support the NFFP value chain such as sustainable production and consumption practices, which will be more profitable for the farmers and consumers. This will be done through capacity building of the stakeholders and the development of a market system that is inclusive and equitable to all.

### Unit Activity 2.1.2

The programme will support the establishment and operationalization of platforms and coordination mechanisms at national and sub-national levels to ensure the sustainability of the NFFP value chain. The focus will be placed on the establishment and operationalization of the platform at national level, which will be supported by the sub-national platforms. The objective of this activity is to support the development of a sustainable market system that enhances access to markets for farmers and other actors in the NFFP value chain.

### Unit Activity 2.1.3

Supporting governments to establish enabling policies, measures and incentives to promote the NFFP activities to benefit from the programme. The benefits of reducing greenhouse gas emissions will be highlighted and the need for sustainable production practices will be emphasized. The programme will support the development of a market system that is inclusive and equitable to all.

### Unit Activity 2.1.4

The programme will focus on technical assistance to existing Producer Organizations (POs) and Multi-Stakeholder Platforms (MSPs) to strengthen their capacity to engage in sustainable production practices. The programme will support the development of a market system that is inclusive and equitable to all.

### Unit Activity 2.1.5

The programme will also provide technical assistance to develop local markets and support the integration of NFFP activities into national policies and strategies. The programme will support the development of a market system that is inclusive and equitable to all.

### Unit Activity 2.1.6

Training and coaching local POs and MSPs in sustainable production and collection practices to enhance their capacity to engage in sustainable production practices. The programme will support the development of a market system that is inclusive and equitable to all.

## B

### 90.
This document is intended to assist the beneficiary countries through their designated entities in the identification, validation and preparation of norms and standards for Non-Transferable Travel Products (NTTP) that will guide the development of NTTPs. The document is structured as follows:
- **Introduction:** Provides an overview of the purpose and scope of the document.
- **Norms and Standards:** Outlines the norms and standards to be followed in the development of NTTPs.
- **Guidelines for Implementation:** Offers guidance on how to implement the norms and standards.

Some GCF countries are more advanced in terms of being, or have standards that are already aligned with the reduction strategies outlined in their NDCs. These countries may wish to use this document as a reference point to develop NTTPs that align with their national objectives. Other countries may be at an earlier stage and will need to start from scratch.

### 91.
The programme will also coordinate with research institutes in order to identify properties of the various NTTP products, the environmental impacts associated with them, and the potential for substitution or improvement. The programme will work closely with the designated entities to ensure that the norms and standards are implemented effectively and consistently across all countries.

### 92.
The programme will facilitate knowledge exchange between NTTP producers and buyers through information sessions and workshops. This will help to identify best practices and promote the adoption of sustainable practices among NTTP producers. The programme will also work with research institutes to develop new technologies and standards for NTTP production.

### 93.
Activity 2.2.5: Identify, from an ex-ante analysis, the potential for emissions reductions through the implementation of emission-reducing technologies and equipment to enhance NTTP production.

#### 93.1.
The programme will work with designated entities to conduct an ex-ante analysis of the potential for emissions reductions through the adoption of new technologies and equipment. This analysis will help to identify the most effective ways to reduce greenhouse gas emissions from NTTP production. The programme will also work with research institutes to develop new technologies and standards for NTTP production.

#### 93.2.
The programme will also work with designated entities to implement the findings of the ex-ante analysis. This will involve the adoption of new technologies and equipment, as well as the development of new norms and standards for NTTP production. The programme will also work with research institutes to monitor and evaluate the effectiveness of the new technologies and equipment.

#### 93.3.
The programme will also work with designated entities to promote the adoption of new technologies and equipment among NTTP producers. This will involve the development of awareness-raising campaigns, as well as the provision of technical assistance and training to help NTTP producers adopt the new technologies and equipment.

### Output 2.3: Establish credit and insurance products designed for an enabling NTFPs value chain actors

#### 95

This output aims to ease the business framework around issues to finance for smallholder farmers, producer associations, and local communities that are involved in the processing of NTFPs. The objective is to develop a financial product portfolio that can be accessed by the GCP, native communities, and private sector actors for processing NTFPs. This will include the following activities:

- **Activity 2.3.1:** Develop a financial product portfolio for small-scale value chain actors.
- **Activity 2.3.2:** Establish credit lines with local banks to finance small-scale value chain actors.

#### 96

Partners will be selected through an open call process, and the activities will be implemented in collaboration with local financial institutions. The activities will be conducted under the Green Climate Fund (GCF) Readiness Accreditation and the National Designated Authority (NDA).

### Activity 2.3.1: Develop a financial product portfolio for small-scale value chain actors

#### 97

This activity aims to develop a portfolio of financial products designed to finance small-scale value chain actors in financial institutions' offerings, enhance financial inclusion, and promote sustainable development.

#### 98

Activities under Output 2.3 include facilitating access to finance through demand-driven solutions with interventions that are tailored to the needs of small-scale value chain actors.

## Activity 3.3.3: Pilot schemes to credit through digital financial solutions and at least one entity and facilitate scale-up

### 09
This activity aims to increase access to finance, particularly in rural and remote areas where the distance to a bank branch is a barrier to accessing formal credit. This approach will be implemented by the National Green Climate Fund (NGCF) to provide small-scale loans to the agriculture sector. The NGCF will also support the development of digital financial solutions for the agriculture sector.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.31.5 - PAGE 8

## Objective: The overall objective is to support countries in their efforts to achieve a level playing field. Firstly, the capacity development actions launched on monitoring and evaluation will mobilize the local knowledge digitalization and the use of data for decision making. Secondly, the project will contribute to the achievement of the Paris Agreement goals by supporting the implementation of Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs) and enhancing the capacity of countries to access climate finance.

### Output 1.1: DIGI-OD and monitoring system enabling systems at national and regional level designed and furnished

#### 1.1.1: The project will support the development of a digital platform for monitoring, reporting and verification (MRV) of GHG emissions and removals. This will include the establishment of a national registry to track GHG emissions and removals, as well as the development of tools and methodologies for data collection and analysis.

#### 1.1.2: The project will also support the development of a monitoring system for adaptation measures. This will include the establishment of a national registry to track adaptation measures, as well as the development of tools and methodologies for data collection and analysis.

### Output 1.2: Capacity building on MRV systems and data management

#### 1.2.1: The project will support the development of a training program on MRV systems and data management. This will include the provision of training materials, as well as the organization of workshops and seminars to build capacity among national stakeholders.

#### 1.2.2: The project will also support the establishment of a network of experts in MRV systems and data management. This will include the facilitation of exchanges between experts from different countries, as well as the provision of technical assistance to countries that need it.

### Output 1.3: Support to NDC implementation

#### 1.3.1: The project will support the development of a roadmap for the implementation of NDCs. This will include the identification of priority actions and the mobilization of resources to support their implementation.

#### 1.3.2: The project will also support the monitoring and evaluation of NDC implementation. This will include the establishment of indicators to track progress, as well as the provision of technical assistance to countries that need it.

### Output 1.4: Support to adaptation planning

#### 1.4.1: The project will support the development of a national adaptation plan (NAP). This will include the identification of priority actions and the mobilization of resources to support their implementation.

#### 1.4.2: The project will also support the monitoring and evaluation of adaptation measures. This will include the establishment of indicators to track progress, as well as the provision of technical assistance to countries that need it.

### Output 1.5: Support to climate finance access

#### 1.5.1: The project will support the development of a strategy for accessing climate finance. This will include the identification of priority actions and the mobilization of resources to support their implementation.

#### 1.5.2: The project will also support the provision of technical assistance to countries that need it. This will include the provision of training, as well as the facilitation of exchanges between experts from different countries.

contains 11 (of a recommended 16) technical monitoring of restoration sites as defined in Government's Guidelines and 20 (out of 40) activities which will not be paid for.

### 1.1

Under the programme, funding and provision will also be provided on basis for accounting GCF impacts per country. This will be done through a national system of monitoring and reporting. The GCF will help to achieve Climate Action Targets (CATs), which are long-term targets set by a country to reduce emissions or increase carbon sinks. These targets are part of the Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs) under the Paris Agreement. The GCF will also support countries in monitoring the progress of their climate actions, which is an essential component of the GCF results-based approach. This will help countries to track their progress and make adjustments as needed.

### Activity 3.1.1: Development, testing and deployment of National GCW land restoration monitoring tools and systems

The programme will involve setting up monitoring mechanisms and tools based on the PASTORI and other relevant tools. This will include the design of indicators and methods for collecting data and processing it into information that can be used to monitor progress towards NDCs and other climate change goals.

### Activity 3.1.2: Transfer knowledge and skills for the use of the GCW Land restoration monitoring system by countries

The programme will involve capacity building, training and support for countries to use the GCW land restoration monitoring tools and systems. This will include the development of training materials and the provision of technical assistance.

### Activity 3.1.3: Development and deployment of regional multi-stakeholder Monitoring platform

The programme will also involve the development and deployment of a regional multi-stakeholder monitoring platform. This will be essential for a more transparent sharing of information among countries, governments and regional stakeholders. The programme will also support the GCW to develop and implement a regional monitoring system that is consistent with the requirements of the Paris Agreement.

The programme will also involve the development of a regional multi-stakeholder monitoring platform. This will be essential for a more transparent sharing of information among countries, governments and regional stakeholders. The programme will also support the GCW to develop and implement a regional monitoring system that is consistent with the requirements of the Paris Agreement.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROTOCOL V.31.1 - PAGE 8

## Activity 3.1.4: Building the capacity of NAGGOW and PAQQW to establish databases of emerging reafforestation and reforestation projects

### Objective

To build the capacity of NAGGOW and PAQQW in establishing, managing, and maintaining databases for emerging reafforestation and reforestation projects. This will enable them to develop and maintain a database that is accessible and user-friendly.

### Activity 3.1.4.1: Establishing a monitoring system

With the establishment of a monitoring system, NAGGOW and PAQQW will be able to track the progress of reafforestation and reforestation projects. This will enable them to identify areas that need attention and provide feedback to project managers.

### Activity 3.1.4.2: Developing a database

NAGGOW and PAQQW will develop a database that includes information on reafforestation and reforestation projects. The database will be designed to be user-friendly and accessible to all stakeholders involved in the projects.

## Activity 3.1.5: Preparing regulatory conditions framework for the approval of all GDD funded interventions in NAGGOW and PAQQW

### Objective

To develop a regulatory framework that outlines the conditions under which reafforestation and reforestation projects can be approved.

### Activity 3.1.5.1: Developing a regulatory framework

NAGGOW and PAQQW will work with relevant government agencies to develop a regulatory framework for approving reafforestation and reforestation projects. The framework will include guidelines on the types of projects that can be funded, the criteria for approval, and the monitoring and evaluation process.

### Activity 3.1.5.2: Engaging stakeholders

NAGGOW and PAQQW will engage with relevant stakeholders to ensure that the regulatory framework is comprehensive and effective. This will include consultations with government agencies, non-governmental organizations, and community groups.

## Activity 3.1.6: Strengthening the capacity of NAGGOW and PAQQW to manage reafforestation and reforestation projects

### Objective

To build the capacity of NAGGOW and PAQQW in managing reafforestation and reforestation projects effectively.

### Activity 3.1.6.1: Developing a management system

NAGGOW and PAQQW will develop a management system that includes guidelines on project planning, implementation, monitoring, and evaluation. The system will be designed to ensure that projects are implemented efficiently and effectively.

### Activity 3.1.6.2: Providing training

NAGGOW and PAQQW will provide training to staff on the use of the management system. This will include training on project planning, implementation, monitoring, and evaluation.

## Activity 3.1.7: Strengthening the capacity of NAGGOW and PAQQW to access climate finance

### Objective

To build the capacity of NAGGOW and PAQQW in accessing climate finance for reafforestation and reforestation projects.

### Activity 3.1.7.1: Developing a funding strategy

NAGGOW and PAQQW will develop a funding strategy that outlines the types of climate finance available, the criteria for eligibility, and the process for applying for funds. The strategy will be designed to ensure that NAGGOW and PAQQW can access the funding they need to implement reafforestation and reforestation projects.

### Activity 3.1.7.2: Providing training

NAGGOW and PAQQW will provide training to staff on how to access climate finance. This will include training on the types of climate finance available, the criteria for eligibility, and the process for applying for funds.

## B

### National Governance structures for institutional strengthening, means of implementation at the subnational level and regional level to support climate change adaptation and mitigation, and to ensure effective and efficient use of public resources at the appropriate level.

#### Output 3.2: Nations and Regional GGW Institutions planning and coordination capacities strengthened

##### Activity 3.2.1: Establishment National GGW Coalitions to promote coherent coordination and planning at country level

Through this bid, the programme will strengthen the capacity of GGW institutions at the national and regional levels to promote coherent coordination and planning for climate change adaptation and mitigation. The programme will support the establishment of a coalition of national and regional GGW institutions that are empowered by an enabling policy framework. The coalitions will be supported through a combination of technical assistance, capacity building, and advocacy initiatives.

The programme will also support the development of a strategic plan for each coalition to guide their work over the next five years. This will include a comprehensive assessment of the needs and priorities of the GGW institutions in each country, as well as an analysis of the enabling environment for effective coordination and collaboration.

The programme will provide technical assistance to support the establishment of the coalitions, including the development of strategic plans, the identification of priority areas for action, and the provision of training and capacity building activities. The programme will also support the development of advocacy initiatives to promote the importance of coherent coordination and planning at the national and regional levels.

The programme will be implemented in partnership with a range of stakeholders, including government institutions, civil society organizations, and the private sector. This will ensure that the programme is aligned with the priorities and needs of the GGW institutions and that it contributes to the achievement of the overall objectives of the Green Climate Fund.

The programme will also support the development of a monitoring and evaluation framework to track progress towards the achievement of the strategic objectives of the coalitions. This will include the development of indicators to measure the impact of the programme, as well as the provision of regular reports on progress.

#### Activity 3.2.2: Prepare and issue planning and regulatory frameworks for the coordination of all GGW aligned activities

The programme will also support the development of a national framework for the coordination of all GGW aligned activities. This will include the preparation of a national action plan, which will outline the priorities and actions for the implementation of the GGW agenda at the national level.

The programme will provide technical assistance to support the development of the national action plan, including the identification of priority areas for action, the development of indicators to measure progress, and the provision of training and capacity building activities. The programme will also support the development of advocacy initiatives to promote the importance of coherent coordination and planning at the national level.

The programme will be implemented in partnership with a range of stakeholders, including government institutions, civil society organizations, and the private sector. This will ensure that the programme is aligned with the priorities and needs of the GGW institutions and that it contributes to the achievement of the overall objectives of the Green Climate Fund.

The programme will also support the development of a monitoring and evaluation framework to track progress towards the achievement of the strategic objectives of the national action plan. This will include the development of indicators to measure the impact of the programme, as well as the provision of regular reports on progress.

## B

### 14.

The main barriers to adoption of sustainable agriculture include lack of access to credit, inadequate extension services and poor infrastructure. These challenges hinder farmers from adopting new technologies and practices that can improve soil health and increase crop yields. Women are particularly affected by these barriers as they often have less access to resources and decision-making power than men. The Green Climate Fund (GCF) can help address these challenges by providing funding for projects that support sustainable agriculture and capacity building in developing countries.

### 15.

The programme of work in coordination and planning with natural and regional initiatives that strengthen the resilience of vulnerable communities is essential to achieve the objectives of the GCF. National policies should be aligned with the programme of work, and it will include specific actions to address the needs of vulnerable groups. The programme of work will also promote the development of local governance and leadership capacity to manage climate risks and opportunities. It will support the integration of gender-responsive approaches in all activities and ensure that women and men have equal access to resources and decision-making processes.

### Activity 3.3.1

Under the set of activities related to Activity 3.3.1, the programme will facilitate access to international carbon markets for small-scale farmers and indigenous communities. This will include support for the development of a portfolio of projects that can be sold as credits in the voluntary carbon market. The programme will also provide technical assistance to help these communities develop and implement projects that are consistent with their needs and priorities.

Plan the establishment of domestic carbon accounting frameworks that integrates agriculture into national greenhouse gas inventories.

Output 3.4 GCF knowledge management and communication capacities requirements for scaling up

### 19. The following requirements are for consideration as part of this activity:
First: Methods developed, tested and implemented to ensure that the project is designed to achieve its objectives.
Second: Indicators will be provided for evaluation of outcomes and impact achieved; vehicles to document results and lessons learned from implementation will be established; and a plan for sharing activities or experiences and managing and adapting to long-term change strategies for plan implementation.

### 19. The programme will also provide school assistance for reporting on civil society and institution actions that help achieve 3.4.1, to a total of Year 2 and beyond; and will include in monitoring and NTFPs value chain development activities (e.g., 3.4.2).

## Activity 3.4.2: Communication, viability and dissemination of knowledge

### 19. Reporting through Management Information System (MIS), SURMOGR will continue the efforts deployed by IGOREMEF to communicate the actions deployed in the field.
The indicators will be used to monitor the progress of the activities; and the results will be shared through the annual RFA reporting platform.
The programme will be responsible for the development of the MIS.

### 19. Activity 3.4.2 Train communication specialists from GDRP's national structures to implement their communications plans.
This includes developing the capacity to communicate effectively with local communities and stakeholders; and to develop a communication plan that is consistent with the objectives set out in the project document.
The programme will also provide school assistance for reporting on civil society and institution actions that help achieve 3.4.1, to a total of Year 2 and beyond; and will include in monitoring and NTFPs value chain development activities (e.g., 3.4.2).

### 19. The programme will participate in both knowledge and experience sharing training courses due to activity 3.4.2.

## Activity 3.4.4: Downstream, in relation to the different target groups identified, adapted supports and modes of delivery will be developed.
This includes developing communication materials that are tailored to the needs of men, women, youth, local leaders, etc.

### 19. The following requirements will be considered under this activity:
Communication tools will be developed under the activity Communication, viability and dissemination of knowledge (activity 3.4.2); working with partners to develop targeted communication materials; and developing a plan for sharing experiences and lessons learned from the project.

### 19. The programme will also provide school assistance for reporting on civil society and institution actions that help achieve 3.4.1, to a total of Year 2 and beyond; and will include in monitoring and NTFPs value chain development activities (e.g., 3.4.2).

## 8.1 Training arrangements (see 10689, appendix 7 page 5 diagram)

This will include developing training materials and providing technical assistance to support the development of national open data portals.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.31/5 - PAGE 8

## COMMITMENT TO ALIGNING THE STRUCTURE OF THE GOF WITH ITS OPERATIONAL PRINCIPLES AND POLICIES

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a new and evolving institution that has been established to support developing countries in their efforts to address climate change. The GCF will build on the experience with the GCF Accreditation and directly complement and complement various efforts with a long-term vision of moving towards the GCF.

### PROJECT GOVERNANCE

A Regional Programme Steering Committee (RPC) will be set up to provide general oversight and strategic direction for the implementation of the Green Climate Fund (GCF). The RPC will closely work with the National Designated Authorities (NDAs) and Accredited Entities (AEs) in their respective countries. The RPC will also oversee the implementation of the Programme Management Unit (PMU), which is responsible for the overall management, coordination, and implementation of the programme. The PMU will be accountable to the RPC and will work closely with the NDAs and AEs to ensure that the programme is implemented effectively.

The RPC will be composed of representatives from the government, private sector, and civil society organizations. The RPC will provide strategic guidance and oversight for the implementation of the programme. The RPC will also oversee the work of the PMU and will ensure that the programme is implemented in accordance with the GCF's operational principles and policies.

### PROJECT IMPLEMENTATION

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) will be implemented through a series of projects and programmes that are designed to support developing countries in their efforts to address climate change. The GCF will provide funding for projects and programmes that are aligned with the GCF's strategic objectives and operational principles.

The GCF will work closely with the NDAs and AEs to ensure that the programme is implemented effectively. The GCF will also provide technical assistance and capacity building support to the NDAs and AEs to ensure that they have the necessary skills and knowledge to implement the programme effectively.

### PROJECT MONITORING AND EVALUATION

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) will be subject to a comprehensive monitoring and evaluation system that is designed to ensure that the programme is implemented effectively. The GCF will provide regular reports on the progress of the programme to the RPC and other stakeholders. The GCF will also conduct periodic evaluations of the programme to assess its effectiveness and identify areas for improvement.

### PROJECT COMMUNICATION AND OUTREACH

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) will be subject to a comprehensive communication and outreach strategy that is designed to ensure that the programme is widely known and understood by stakeholders. The GCF will provide regular updates on the progress of the programme to the RPC and other stakeholders. The GCF will also conduct outreach activities to raise awareness of the programme and to engage with stakeholders.

### PROJECT FINANCIAL MANAGEMENT

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) will be subject to a comprehensive financial management system that is designed to ensure that the programme is implemented effectively. The GCF will provide regular reports on the financial status of the programme to the RPC and other stakeholders. The GCF will also conduct periodic audits of the programme to assess its financial health and identify areas for improvement.

### PROJECT RISK MANAGEMENT

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) will be subject to a comprehensive risk management system that is designed to ensure that the programme is implemented effectively. The GCF will provide regular reports on the risks associated with the programme to the RPC and other stakeholders. The GCF will also conduct periodic risk assessments of the programme to identify potential risks and develop strategies to mitigate them.

## B

### 108

EUROPEAN COMMISSION will co-finance National Implementing Communities NBCs, a total of 8 regional clusters were selected to be implemented on the ground. The aim is for these clusters to become the NBCs in their respective countries and to be able to provide technical assistance to other countries that are interested in setting up NBCs. This will ensure that the NBCs are owned by the countries and the countries can take over the management of the NBCs once the initial phase has been completed. The EU will continue to support the NBCs during the knowledge phase and will be a differentiator factor for each country. Each of the clusters will have an EU representative on their board.

### Accredited Entities

#### 109

FAO is here as the accredited Entity (AE) for the programme. The multi-country programme supervision function will be carried out by FAO and the National Designated Authorities (NDAs). The NDAs are the entities that have been accredited to manage resources from the GCF. They are responsible for including, at a minimum, approval of project activities, an appropriate technical and financial oversight and reporting on the use of funds. This is outlined in the standard of practice, as well as the functions and quality of services to be provided by the NDAs. The NDAs will also provide guidance to the accredited entities in accordance with the provisions outlined in the Accreditation Master Agreement (AMA) between FAO and GCF.

#### 109

FAO will act as AE and will be accredited by relevant Office and Divisions of FAO headquarters (HQ). In line with its mandate, FAO will provide technical support to the NDAs and the implementing entities. This includes but is not limited to the following:

- Provide technical assistance to the NDAs in the preparation and implementation of projects.
- Provide technical assistance to the implementing entities in the preparation and implementation of projects.
- Provide technical assistance to the implementing entities in the preparation and implementation of projects.

### Executive Entities

#### 130

The programme will be executed by FAO and the Governments of eight countries as Executing Entities. Activities undertaken by GCF process will be monitored by FAO and the Governments of each country. Monitoring and reporting will be carried out in accordance with the provisions outlined in the Accreditation Master Agreement (AMA) between FAO and GCF.

#### 130

The programme will be executed by FAO and the Governments of eight countries as Executing Entities. Activities undertaken by GCF process will be monitored by FAO and the Governments of each country. Monitoring and reporting will be carried out in accordance with the provisions outlined in the Accreditation Master Agreement (AMA) between FAO and GCF.

## Program Management Unit (PMU)
The **Program Management Unit (PMU)** will be responsible for managing the overall methodology programs. The PMU will be composed of a component team based on the FAD and the National Agency for the Climate Change (NACC). The PMU will be responsible for the implementation of the program, including the management of funds, technical assistance, and monitoring and evaluation. The PMU will also be responsible for the overall coordination of the program activities.

## Implementation
The **implementation** of the programs will be undertaken at the country level through County Governments. The NACC will be involved in the National Agency for the Climate Change. A Technical Working Group (TWG) will be established to oversee the implementation of the project. This group will include representatives from a land identification committee, a technical working group, and a climate change committee.

## Funding
The **funding** will be administered through the Ministry of Environment and Energy. The funding will be used for the implementation of the program activities. The FAD and NDAA will implement the project in collaboration with the NACC. The funds will be used to support the implementation of the project, including technical assistance, service providers, and M&E who will set up implementing partners' same services for the project.

## Flow of Funds and Project Implementation
1. **Funding**: The flow with the program implementation arrangements agreed upon the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) and the NACC.
2. **Agreement**: The Project Agreement between FAD and the Government of the Republic of Kenya will be signed.
3. **Documentation**: The Project Document will be prepared by the NACC.

## Flow of funds and implementation arrangements

### Key Stakeholders

- **Governments**
- **National Designated Authorities (NDAs)**
- **Accredited Entities**

### Process Overview

1. **Funding Allocation**: The GCF will allocate resources to countries based on their needs, capacities, and commitments.
2. **Project Preparation**: NDAs and accredited entities work together to prepare projects for funding.
3. **Project Approval**: Projects are approved by the GCF Board after thorough review and assessment.
4. **Funding Disbursement**: Funds are released to implementing entities upon meeting agreed milestones.

### Funding Mechanisms

- **Grants**
- **Loans**

## Financial Additions

The six finances (the eight Government) will be responsible for reporting in the NE & Accord with the GCF's policies and procedures. The GCF will ensure that the funds are used effectively and efficiently, and GCF and FAO Project Agreements to be signed between each government's to enhance and FAO to capacity building and technical assistance.

### FAO's Role

FAO, a specialized agency of the United Nations, is well placed to undertake the planned activities given its proven track record for addressing climate change adaptation and mitigation. Including poverty and food security, and sustainable development. FAO has extensive experience in providing technical support to countries in the design and implementation of projects.

### FAO's Contributions

- **Technical Expertise**: FAO will provide technical expertise and support to governments and implementing entities.
- **Project Preparation**: FAO will assist in preparing project proposals and ensuring they meet GCF requirements.
- **Capacity Building**: FAO will build capacity among NDAs and accredited entities to effectively manage GCF funds.

### Funding Allocation

FAO will work with the GCF to ensure that resources are allocated efficiently and effectively. FAO will also provide technical assistance to governments and implementing entities to enhance their capacity to access and utilize GCF funds.

additional income from a range of promoting products such as beverages, snacks, nuts, fruit, forage crops, etc., can be used to support the development and implementation of the project. The project will be implemented in a manner that is consistent with the principles of the Green Climate Fund, including the following:

- The project will be implemented through the same arrangements through which the Government has been implementing other projects funded by the GCF.

### 1.0 Project Summary

The project is a further developed version of the original proposal submitted to the GCF in March 2018. It has been designed to address the needs of the country through a comprehensive approach that includes:

- The development of a sustainable land management program.
- The establishment of a national agroforestry program.
- The provision of training and capacity building for farmers and communities.
- The promotion of sustainable agriculture practices.

### 2.0 Project Strategy to Meet, 500,000 Tons, approximately 750,000 Tons

The project strategy has developed a clear yet flexible approach for investments. Under component 1, the GCF grant will make just one-time investments in the emission reduction (deforestation) activities with the aim of reducing emissions from deforestation and forest degradation. The project will be implemented through the same arrangements as other projects funded by the Government.

The project will also include a range of activities that are designed to support the development of sustainable agriculture practices, including:

- The promotion of sustainable agriculture practices.
- The provision of training and capacity building for farmers and communities.
- The establishment of a national agroforestry program.
- The provision of training and capacity building for farmers and communities.

- The promotion of sustainable agriculture practices.

- The establishment of a national agroforestry program.

## Type of measurement
### Department of Community Development

| **Programme Implementation** | **End Strategy** | **Measurement** |
|-------------------------------|------------------|-----------------|
| **Village Technician**        | Built capacity    | Programmes are implemented through the use of village technicians. |
| **Renforcement of community structures** | Acceptance with deep understanding and ownership by communities | Once the time is due for implementation, the programme will be implemented by the communities themselves. The programme will be implemented in a way that is sustainable and will continue to be implemented even after the programme has been completed. |
| **Community monitoring on land use** | Sustainable land management practices are adopted | Submitting land-use change reports for interpretation and analysis by the community. |
| **Promotion of measurement standards (Define, Measure, Analyse, Improve)** | The programme will encompass these steps in its implementation process | The programme is supported through a continuous improvement cycle that includes defining, measuring, analysing, and improving processes. |
| **Promotion of needs and concerns** | Mobile community land use monitoring teams are established to promote high quality land management practices | The monitoring group is for the purpose of ensuring that the programme is implemented in accordance with the standards set by the community. |
| **Capacity building to enable use of tools** | A two-day training with practical field exercises | The programme is run through regular meetings in the communities and the use of these tools. |
