# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.31/5 - PAGE 8

## B

### Terms of reference and procedures for preparing the proposal

#### The first priority under this component is to develop a new innovative product based on the existing knowledge of another project in NTFP production and marketing and in-line with engagement at the local level. Local communities will be engaged in the development process, including the identification of potential products, market analysis, and value chain analysis. This will ensure that the product meets the needs of a large NTFP market. The engagement has been based on the fact that local communities have the knowledge to identify potential products and markets. During the term of reference, major criteria include ethical labor practices, environmental sustainability, and social impact assessment. It is expected that the project will engage women and men engaged in collection and marketing their NTFP products. The project will also support the development of a business plan for the new product.

### A range of conventional financial instruments

The second priority under this component is to provide loans to finance the value chain activities from a range of conventional financial instruments. The Global Environment Facility (GEF) will address the needs of small and medium-sized enterprises (SMEs) in the NTFP sector through the provision of loans, grants, and equity investments. This will include the development of a range of financial products that are tailored to the specific needs of SMEs in the NTFP sector. The project will also support the development of a range of financial instruments that are designed to meet the needs of small and medium-sized enterprises (SMEs) in the NTFP sector.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.3.1 PAGE 2

## D.2. Proposals are accepted with any having a total less than $500,000 or in a United States Plan. A budget is required for all proposals with the exception of 1.2.A.1. Proposals for developing countries for needs in LDCs (Least Developed Countries) with the exception of those who have submitted their budgets.

### D.2.1. General Information

#### Description

- **Title:** Green Climate Fund Proposal
- **Submission Deadline:** April 30, 2023
- **Funding Agency:** GCF (Green Climate Fund)
- **Eligibility:** NGOs, NPOs, and IOs of LDCs (Least Developed Countries) with a focus on climate change mitigation and adaptation projects.

#### Funding

- **Total Available Funds:** $500,000
- **Funding Duration:** 1 year
- **Eligible Expenses:** Project implementation costs, personnel expenses, equipment, travel, and administrative costs.

### D.2.2. Application Process

#### Requirements

- **Application Form:** Download from the GCF website.
- **Budget Justification:** Detailed budget breakdown required.
- **Project Narrative:** Maximum 5 pages, including objectives, activities, timeline, and expected outcomes.
- **Supporting Documents:** Letters of support, feasibility studies, and environmental impact assessments.

#### Submission

- **Deadline:** April 30, 2023
- **Submission Method:** Online submission through the GCF portal.

### D.2.3. Proposal Content

Proposals should include essential (max 500 words), approximately 1 page.

There are several key elements included in the GCRACOVA approach which are expected to satisfy the requirements of the Green Climate Fund. These include:

- **Needs Assessment:** Identify and document the needs, vulnerabilities, and local communities.
- **Understanding the Context:** Understand the socio-economic context and governance structures.
- **Governance Approaches:** Expected to provide a supportive framework which will guide local governments in implementing climate change adaptation projects.

### D.2.4. Additional Information

Additional information can be found on the GCF website or by contacting the GCF Secretariat.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal v.3.1

## D. Sustainable development goals

### D.3 Sustainable development goals

The programme is designed to address several of the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) namely:

- **SDG 1**: No Poverty.
- **SDG 2**: Zero Hunger.
- **SDG 5**: Gender Equality.
- **SDG 8**: Decent Work and Economic Growth (SDG 8).
- **SDG 9**: Industry, Innovation and Infrastructure.
- **SDG 10**: Reduced Inequalities.
- **SDG 13**: Climate Action.

The programme will address these SDGs through the following activities:

- **SDG 1 & 2**: The programme will provide employment opportunities for local people in the area. This will help to reduce poverty and hunger by providing income and food security.
- **SDG 5**: The programme will promote gender equality by ensuring that women have equal access to resources and decision-making processes.
- **SDG 8 & 9**: The programme will create jobs through the establishment of a nursery, agroforestry, and reforestation activities. This will also support innovation and infrastructure development in the area.
- **SDG 10**: The programme will reduce inequalities by providing equal access to resources and opportunities for all community members regardless of their socio-economic status.
- **SDG 13**: The programme will contribute to climate action by promoting sustainable land use practices, reforestation, and agroforestry.

### D.4 Environmental benefits

The programme is designed to help secure several environmental benefits including:

- **Carbon sequestration**: The programme will promote the planting of trees which will help to absorb carbon dioxide from the atmosphere.
- **Soil conservation**: The programme will promote sustainable land use practices that will help to prevent soil erosion and degradation.
- **Water management**: The programme will promote the efficient use of water resources through the establishment of irrigation systems and rainwater harvesting techniques.

### D.5 Social benefits including food security

Around a center of the 100 million people who live in the area, the programme will provide employment opportunities for local people. This will help to reduce poverty and hunger by providing income and food security. # Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal v.1.0 - PAGE 8

## Background

**Reference:** [Green Climate Fund](https://www.greenclimate.fund)

The Saudi countries are the emission sources. Nevertheless, each has made a commitment to take action towards reducing their emissions. The Kingdom of Saudi Arabia (KSA) is one of the largest contributors to the global climate change issue. The KSA government has committed to reduce its greenhouse gas emissions by 130 million tons of CO2 equivalent annually by 2030, which is expected to be achieved through a combination of measures including renewable energy, energy efficiency, and carbon capture and storage (CCS). The KSA government has also committed to increasing the share of renewable energy in the total energy mix to 50% by 2030.

### Table: Current Emissions and Commitments

| Country/Region | Current Emissions (MtCO2e) | Target Emissions (MtCO2e) | Timeline |
|----------------|---------------------------|--------------------------|---------|
| KSA            | 650                       | 520                      | 2030    |
| UAE            | 280                       | 190                      | 2030    |
| Qatar           | 140                       | 90                       | 2030    |

### Details

The KSA government has outlined several initiatives to achieve its emissions reduction targets. These include:

- **Renewable Energy:** Increasing the share of renewable energy in the total energy mix.
- **Energy Efficiency:** Implementing energy efficiency measures across various sectors.
- **Carbon Capture and Storage (CCS):** Utilizing CCS technology to reduce emissions from industrial processes.

The KSA government has also committed to investing in research and development of new technologies that can help reduce greenhouse gas emissions. The government is working with international organizations such as the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) to achieve its goals.

### Funding Requirements

The KSA government is seeking funding to support its climate change initiatives. The total estimated cost for these projects is $10 billion over a period of 5 years. The funds will be used to:

- **Renewable Energy Projects:** $4 billion
- **Energy Efficiency Programs:** $3 billion
- **Carbon Capture and Storage (CCS):** $2 billion
- **Research and Development:** $1 billion

### Timeline

The funding is required by the end of 2023 to ensure that the projects can be implemented in a timely manner. The KSA government is committed to providing regular updates on the progress of these initiatives.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.3.1: PAGE 81

## Summary

### Objective

The objective of this proposal is to leverage and scale up the impact of the Green Climate Fund (GCF) in supporting climate change mitigation and adaptation projects.

### Background

The GCF was established by the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) in 2010. It aims to support developing countries in their efforts to mitigate and adapt to climate change.

## Proposal Details

### Project Title

**Enhancing Resilience through Integrated Urban Infrastructure**

### Project Description

This project aims to enhance the resilience of urban infrastructure in vulnerable cities by integrating climate-smart solutions. The project will focus on improving water management, green spaces, and sustainable transportation systems.

### Funding Requirements

- **Total GCF Funding Requested:** USD 10 million
- **Co-financing:** USD 5 million from national government and private sector contributions.
- **Project Duration:** 5 years (2023-2028)

### Project Components

1. **Water Management**
   - Implementation of rainwater harvesting systems
   - Restoration of wetlands and water bodies
   - Improvement of drainage infrastructure

2. **Green Spaces**
   - Establishment of urban forests and parks
   - Greening of rooftops and walls
   - Promotion of green corridors

3. **Sustainable Transportation**
   - Development of bike lanes and pedestrian paths
   - Introduction of electric public transportation systems
   - Improvement of traffic management systems

### Implementation Strategy

- **Phase I (Year 1):** Conduct baseline studies, develop project designs, and engage stakeholders.
- **Phase II (Years 2-4):** Implement project activities and monitor progress.
- **Phase III (Year 5):** Evaluate outcomes and document lessons learned.

## Approval Through GCF Board Meeting 30/99

### Decision Points

- **Approval of Project:** GCF Board to approve the project with a total funding of USD 15 million (USD 10 million from GCF and USD 5 million co-financing).
- **Disbursement Schedule:**
  - USD 2.5 million in Year 1
  - USD 3.5 million in Year 2
  - USD 4 million in Year 3
  - USD 3 million in Year 4
  - USD 2 million in Year 5

### Financial Management

- **Funding Agency:** Green Climate Fund (GCF)
- **Implementation Agency:** National Development Bank (NDB)

## Additional Information

### Project Impact

- **Reduction of GHG Emissions:** 10,000 tons per year
- **Improvement in Urban Biodiversity:** 20% increase in green spaces
- **Enhancement of Livelihoods:** 5,000 jobs created through project activities

### Project Timeline

- **Start Date:** January 1, 2023
- **End Date:** December 31, 2028

## Notes

- **Project Lead:** Jane Doe, Senior Project Manager, NDB
- **Contact Information:**
  - Email: jane.doe@ndb.gov
  - Phone: +1 (123) 456-7890

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V1.0 - PAGE 8

![Green Climate Fund Logo](https://via.placeholder.com/50)

## Project Title: Renewable Energy Accessibility Enhancement Initiative

### Project Description:
The Renewable Energy Accessibility Enhancement Initiative aims to increase access to renewable energy sources for underserved communities. This project will focus on installing solar panels and wind turbines in rural areas, providing clean and sustainable energy solutions.

### Objectives:
- Increase access to renewable energy by 30% within the next two years.
- Reduce carbon emissions by 25% through the adoption of renewable energy sources.
- Promote economic development by creating local jobs in the renewable energy sector.

### Budget Summary:

| Item                      | Quantity | Unit Cost (USD) | Total Cost (USD) |
|---------------------------|----------|-----------------|------------------|
| Solar Panels              | 1,000    | 500             | 500,000          |
| Wind Turbines             | 20       | 80,000          | 1,600,000        |
| Installation Costs         | -        | -               | 300,000          |
| **Total**                 |          |                 | **2,400,000**    |

### Funding Request:

- **Requested Amount:** USD 1,871,456
- **Duration:** 2 years

### Implementation Plan:
The project will be implemented in three phases over a two-year period. Phase I will involve the procurement of materials and equipment. Phase II will focus on the installation of solar panels and wind turbines. Phase III will include training for local communities on the use and maintenance of renewable energy systems.

### Complementary Activities:
- Conduct workshops to educate communities about the benefits of renewable energy.
- Partner with local schools to integrate renewable energy lessons into the curriculum.
- Collaborate with other NGOs to expand the reach of the project.

### Associated Risks:
- Delays in procurement due to supply chain issues.
- Potential resistance from communities unfamiliar with renewable energy.
- Weather-related disruptions during installation phases.

### Supporting Documents:
- Environmental Impact Assessment
- Community Engagement Plan

### Annexure: List of Materials

1. Solar Panels
2. Wind Turbines
3. Installation Equipment
4. Safety Gear

### Mitigation

- **Activity:** Renewable Energy Project
- **Objective:** To reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 50% within five years.
- **Location:** Urban and rural areas of Country X.
- **Beneficiaries:** Local communities, businesses, and government agencies.

### Adaptation

- **Activity:** Climate Resilient Infrastructure
- **Objective:** To enhance the resilience of infrastructure to climate change impacts.
- **Location:** Coastal regions and flood-prone areas of Country X.
- **Beneficiaries:** Local communities, schools, hospitals, and critical infrastructure.

## Implementation

### Mitigation Activities

1. **Renewable Energy Project**
   - **Objective:** To install solar panels on public buildings.
   - **Actions:**
     - Conduct feasibility studies.
     - Procure and install solar panels.
     - Monitor and maintain the systems.
   - **Timeline:** 2 years.

2. **Energy Efficiency Program**
   - **Objective:** To promote energy-efficient appliances.
   - **Actions:**
     - Launch awareness campaigns.
     - Provide subsidies for energy-efficient appliances.
     - Monitor usage and impact.
   - **Timeline:** 3 years.

### Adaptation Activities

1. **Climate Resilient Infrastructure**
   - **Objective:** To build flood-resistant schools.
   - **Actions:**
     - Conduct vulnerability assessments.
     - Design and construct flood-resistant buildings.
     - Provide training for maintenance.
   - **Timeline:** 2 years.

2. **Early Warning Systems**
   - **Objective:** To establish an early warning system for floods.
   - **Actions:**
     - Install sensors and monitoring equipment.
     - Develop a warning dissemination system.
     - Conduct regular drills and training.
   - **Timeline:** 1 year.

- **Objective:** To enhance the capacity of local governments to manage climate change projects.

  - Organize workshops and training sessions.
  - Provide technical assistance and resources.
  - Facilitate knowledge sharing among stakeholders.
- **Timeline:** Ongoing.

| **Project Title** | **Project Description** | **Budget Allocation** |
|-------------------|-------------------------|-----------------------|
| **Community Outreach Program** | Annual outreach program to educate the local community on climate change and sustainability. | $300,000 |
| **Carbon Offsets Initiative** | Annual initiative to offset carbon emissions through reforestation projects. | $400,000 |

| **Project Title** | **Project Description** | **Budget Allocation** |
|-------------------|-------------------------|-----------------------|
| **Green Building Certification** | Obtaining LEED certification for all new buildings constructed within the facility. | $250,000 |
| **Energy Audit** | Annual energy audit to identify and implement further efficiency measures. | $150,000 |

---

Note: 1% allocation of GEF-Northern Dimension Vegetation Index value in unplanted and no cropped areas within the GDR area of the facility will be used for climate change adaptation projects. Emphasis will be placed on the use of native species for revegetation and restoration, and the establishment of monitoring protocols to assess the success of these initiatives.

## Section I: Background and Rationale for Proposal

### 1.4.1 % of GCF Allocated to Adaptation (2015-2020)

- **Total GCF Allocation:** $10 billion
- **Adaptation Allocation:** $2 billion
- **Percentage:** 20%

### 1.4.2 Breakdown of Adaptation Funding

#### Annual Programming (2015-2020)

| Year | Amount Allocated |
| --- | --- |
| 2015 | $679 million |
| 2016 | $813 million |
| 2017 | $745 million |
| 2018 | $789 million |
| 2019 | $795 million |
| 2020 | $776 million |

#### Annual Programming (2021-2025)

| Year | Amount Allocated |
| --- | --- |
| 2021 | $843 million |
| 2022 | $895 million |
| 2023 | $917 million |
| 2024 | $936 million |
| 2025 | $954 million |

### Section II: Proposed Activities and Budget

#### Activity I.1: National Adaptation Planning (NAP)

- **Objective:** Integrate climate change adaptation into national policies
- **Budget:** $500 million

#### Activity I.2: Regional Outreach and Technical Assistance

- **Objective:** Provide technical support to regional initiatives
- **Budget:** $300 million

#### Activity II.1: Early Warning Systems (EWS)

- **Objective:** Enhance early warning systems for vulnerable communities
- **Budget:** $750 million

#### Activity II.2: Resilience Building in Key Sectors

- **Objective:** Strengthen resilience in agriculture, water management, and health sectors
- **Budget:** $600 million

### Section III: Monitoring and Evaluation Framework

#### A. Performance Indicators

1. Percentage of countries with approved NAPs
2. Number of regional initiatives supported
3. Reduction in disaster-related fatalities due to EWS
4. Increase in resilience index scores for key sectors

#### B. Reporting Requirements

- Annual progress reports
- Mid-term evaluation (2023)
- Final evaluation (2025)

### Section IV: Stakeholder Engagement and Communication Strategy

1. Regular consultations with vulnerable communities
2. Collaboration with regional and international organizations
3. Public awareness campaigns to highlight success stories

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v1.0

## PAGE 109

---

**GREEN CLIMATE FUND**

- [ ] **Project Title:** Green Climate Initiative
- [ ] **Submitted By:** John Doe
- [ ] **Date:** October 5, 2023
- [ ] **Contact Information:**
  - Email: john.doe@example.com
  - Phone: (123) 456-7890

---

**PROJECT OVERVIEW**

The Green Climate Initiative aims to combat climate change by promoting sustainable practices and renewable energy sources. This proposal outlines the objectives, strategies, and budget for implementing green projects globally.

### Objectives

1. **Reduce Carbon Emissions:** Decrease global carbon emissions by 20% within five years.
2. **Promote Renewable Energy:** Increase the adoption of renewable energy sources to 30% of total energy consumption.
3. **Educate and Advocate:** Raise awareness about climate change through educational programs and advocacy campaigns.

### Strategies

1. **Renewable Energy Projects:**
   - Invest in solar, wind, and hydroelectric power plants.
   - Provide grants for small-scale renewable energy projects.

2. **Energy Efficiency Programs:**
   - Implement energy-efficient technologies in industries and households.
   - Offer incentives for reducing energy consumption.

3. **Education and Advocacy:**
   - Develop educational materials and workshops on climate change.
   - Collaborate with NGOs to raise awareness through campaigns and media outreach.

### Budget

- **Total Budget:** $5,000,000
  - **Renewable Energy Projects:** $2,500,000
  - **Energy Efficiency Programs:** $1,500,000
  - **Education and Advocacy:** $1,000,000

---

**CONTACT INFORMATION**

For more information or to support the Green Climate Initiative, please contact:

- Email: [info@greenclimate.org](mailto:info@greenclimate.org)
- Website: [www.greenclimate.org](http://www.greenclimate.org)

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL v1.0 | PAGE 101

## Objective 5: Support Climate Change Mitigation and Adaptation in Developing Countries

### Annual Performance Indicators

| Category | Indicator Code | Description of the Indicator | Source of Data | Frequency of Reporting |
|----------|-----------------|-------------------------------|-----------------|------------------------|
| **Greenhouse Gas Emissions** | GCF.01 | Total GHG emissions (MtCO2e) reported by countries | National GHG inventories and UNFCCC submissions | Annually |
| **Renewable Energy Capacity** | RE.CAP | Installed capacity of renewable energy projects (MW) | Project reports and national energy statistics | Quarterly |
| **Climate Finance Disbursements** | CF.DIS | Total climate finance disbursed to developing countries (USD million) | GCF reporting system and project documents | Annually |
| **Adaptation Projects Implemented** | ADAPT.PROJ | Number of adaptation projects completed | Project completion reports | Annually |

### Special Conditions

- The data for renewable energy capacity should include both grid-connected and off-grid systems.
- Climate finance disbursements should be reported in accordance with the GCF reporting guidelines.
- Adaptation projects must meet the criteria set by the GCF for project approval.

## Objective 6: Enhance Capacity Building and Knowledge Sharing

### Annual Performance Indicators

| Category | Indicator Code | Description of the Indicator | Source of Data | Frequency of Reporting |
|----------|-----------------|-------------------------------|-----------------|------------------------|
| **Training Programs** | TRAIN.PROG | Number of training programs conducted | Training program reports and participant lists | Quarterly |
| **Workshops and Conferences** | WORKSHOP.CONF | Number of workshops and conferences organized | Event reports and attendance records | Annually |
| **Publications and Research** | PUB.RES | Number of publications and research papers produced | Publication databases and research outputs | Annually |
| **Knowledge Sharing Platforms** | KNOW.SHARE | Number of active knowledge sharing platforms | Platform usage statistics and user feedback | Quarterly |

### Special Conditions

- Training programs should cover a range of topics including climate change mitigation, adaptation, and sustainable development.
- Workshops and conferences must include participation from both developed and developing countries.
- Publications and research should be peer-reviewed and widely disseminated.

## Objective 7: Promote Gender Equality and Social Inclusion

### Annual Performance Indicators

| Category | Indicator Code | Description of the Indicator | Source of Data | Frequency of Reporting |
|----------|-----------------|-------------------------------|-----------------|------------------------|
| **Gender Balance** | GEN.BAL | Percentage of women in leadership positions within GCF projects | Project reports and gender audits | Annually |
| **Social Inclusion** | SOC.INCL | Number of socially inclusive activities implemented | Social inclusion impact assessments | Annually |
| **Access to Climate Finance** | ACC.CLIM.FIN | Proportion of climate finance reaching vulnerable groups | Project reports and beneficiary surveys | Annually |

### Special Conditions

- Gender balance should be reported at both the project level and organizational level.
- Social inclusion activities must address the needs of marginalized communities, including indigenous peoples and persons with disabilities.
- Access to climate finance data should include disaggregated information by gender and socio-economic status.

## Objective 8: Strengthen Institutional Arrangements

### Annual Performance Indicators

| Category | Indicator Code | Description of the Indicator | Source of Data | Frequency of Reporting |
|----------|-----------------|-------------------------------|-----------------|------------------------|
| **Board Meetings** | BOARD.MEET | Number of board meetings held | Board meeting minutes and agendas | Annually |
| **Policy Development** | POL.DEV | Number of policies and guidelines developed or updated | Policy documents and internal reports | Annually |
| **Staff Training** | STA.TRAIN | Number of staff training programs conducted | Training program reports | Quarterly |

### Special Conditions

- Board meetings should include representation from both developed and developing countries.
- Policy development must align with the GCF's strategic objectives and international climate change agreements.
- Staff training programs should cover areas such as project management, financial reporting, and gender mainstreaming.

## Objective 9: Ensure Environmental and Social Safeguards

### Annual Performance Indicators

| Category | Indicator Code | Description of the Indicator | Source of Data | Frequency of Reporting |
|----------|-----------------|-------------------------------|-----------------|------------------------|
| **Environmental Impact Assessments** | EIA | Number of environmental impact assessments conducted | Project reports and EIA documents | Annually |
| **Social Impact Assessments** | SIA | Number of social impact assessments conducted | Project reports and SIA documents | Annually |
| **Compliance with Safeguards** | COMP.SAFE | Percentage of projects compliant with GCF safeguard policies | Compliance audit reports | Annually |

### Special Conditions

- Environmental and social impact assessments must be conducted in accordance with international standards.
- Compliance with safeguards should include regular audits and third-party verifications.
- Projects must adhere to the GCF's environmental and social safeguard policies.

## Objective 10: Enhance Transparency and Accountability

### Annual Performance Indicators

| Category | Indicator Code | Description of the Indicator | Source of Data | Frequency of Reporting |
|----------|-----------------|-------------------------------|-----------------|------------------------|
| **Public Disclosure** | PUB.DISC | Percentage of projects with public disclosure reports | Project reports and public disclosure databases | Annually |
| **Stakeholder Consultation** | STA.CONS | Number of stakeholder consultations conducted | Consultation reports and participant lists | Quarterly |
| **Internal Audits** | INT.AUDIT | Number of internal audits conducted | Audit reports | Annually |

### Special Conditions

- Public disclosure reports should be made available on the GCF website and other relevant platforms.
- Stakeholder consultation must include input from local communities, civil society organizations, and private sector entities.
- Internal audits should cover areas such as financial management, project implementation, and compliance with safeguards.

## Project programs to benefit indicators

|-------------|-----------|--------|----------|-----------------|--------------|------------|
| Co-benefit 1: Health benefits from reduced air pollution | The number of avoided premature deaths due to improved air quality | Annual average concentration of PM2.5 | Baseline (2019) | 10% reduction by 2030 | 50% reduction by 2050 | A. This is based on the World Health Organization's estimates of the health impacts of air pollution. B. The baseline value is taken from the most recent available data. |
| Co-benefit 2: Economic benefits from reduced healthcare costs | Annual savings in healthcare costs due to improved air quality | Annual average concentration of PM2.5 | Baseline (2019) | 15% reduction by 2030 | 40% reduction by 2050 | A. This is based on the World Health Organization's estimates of the economic impacts of air pollution. B. The baseline value is taken from the most recent available data. |
| Co-benefit 3: Educational benefits from reduced absenteeism | Annual days of school missed due to respiratory illnesses | Annual average concentration of PM2.5 | Baseline (2019) | 10% reduction by 2030 | 30% reduction by 2050 | A. This is based on the World Health Organization's estimates of the educational impacts of air pollution. B. The baseline value is taken from the most recent available data. |

## Notes

The GCF seeks to fund the best and most sustainable projects that contribute to climate change mitigation and adaptation. Projects must be consistent with the GCF's environmental and social safeguards, including those related to gender equality and social inclusion.

For more information on the GCF's funding process, please visit our website at [www.greenclimate.fund](http://www.greenclimate.fund). You can also contact us directly by emailing info@greenclimate.fund or calling +1 (800) 555-2369.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V1.0 PAGE 109

## Objectives of the Proposed Project

### Objective 1: Climate Change Mitigation
- **Description**: Reduce greenhouse gas emissions by promoting energy efficiency and renewable energy.
- **Activities**:
  - Implement energy-efficient technologies in industries.
  - Promote the use of renewable energy sources.

### Objective 2: Climate Change Adaptation
- **Description**: Enhance resilience to climate change impacts through adaptation measures.
- **Activities**:
  - Develop early warning systems for extreme weather events.
  - Implement climate-resilient infrastructure.

## E.A. Programme Activities and Deliverables

### Activities (IMF All functions)
- **Sub-activities** | **Description** | **Deliverables**
- **E.A.1.1: Development of Climate Change Mitigation Strategies** |
  - Conduct a comprehensive assessment of greenhouse gas emissions.
  - Develop mitigation strategies tailored to the local context.
  - Create an action plan for implementing mitigation measures.

### E.A.1.2: Implementation of Mitigation Measures
- **Description**: Implement identified mitigation measures and monitor their effectiveness.
- **Sub-activities**:
  - **E.A.1.2.1: Energy Efficiency Projects** |
    - Identify energy-intensive sectors.
    - Develop and implement energy efficiency projects.
    - Monitor and evaluate the impact of these projects.

  - **E.A.1.2.2: Renewable Energy Projects** |
    - Conduct feasibility studies for renewable energy projects.
    - Develop and implement renewable energy projects.
    - Monitor and evaluate the impact of these projects.

### E.A.2: Development of Climate Change Adaptation Strategies
- **Description**: Develop strategies to enhance resilience to climate change impacts.
- **Sub-activities**:
  - **E.A.2.1: Early Warning Systems** |
    - Develop early warning systems for extreme weather events.
    - Implement and maintain these systems.

  - **E.A.2.2: Climate-Resilient Infrastructure** |
    - Identify vulnerable infrastructure.
    - Develop and implement climate-resilient infrastructure projects.
    - Monitor and evaluate the impact of these projects.

## Notes

- The project will be implemented over a period of five years, with an initial focus on energy efficiency and renewable energy projects.
- The project will be funded by the Green Climate Fund (GCF) and implemented in collaboration with relevant national and local stakeholders.
- The project aims to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 20% within the next five years.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V1.0: PAGE 109

## Objective

To ensure the effective implementation of activities that will contribute to the achievement of the Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs) and the Paris Agreement, with a focus on enhancing climate resilience and reducing greenhouse gas emissions.

### Activities

#### Activity 1.1.1: Training and Capacity Building

**Description:** Conduct training programs for local government officials and community leaders on climate change adaptation and mitigation strategies.

**Sub-activities:**

- 1.1.1.1: Training of trainers (ToT) in climate change adaptation.
- 1.1.1.2: Development of training materials and resources.
- 1.1.1.3: Conducting workshops and seminars on climate change mitigation.

**Deliverables:**

- 1.1.1.1: 50 local government officials trained as trainers.
- 1.1.1.2: Training materials developed and distributed to 100 participants.
- 1.1.1.3: 10 workshops conducted, reaching 500 community leaders.

#### Activity 1.1.2: Technical Assistance

**Description:** Provide technical assistance to local governments in the implementation of climate change adaptation and mitigation projects.

**Sub-activities:**

- 1.1.2.1: Selection and engagement of technical experts.
- 1.1.2.2: Development of project proposals.
- 1.1.2.3: Monitoring and evaluation of project progress.

**Deliverables:**

- 1.1.2.1: 5 technical experts engaged for a period of 6 months.
- 1.1.2.2: 3 project proposals developed and submitted to funding agencies.
- 1.1.2.3: Quarterly reports on project progress submitted to the GCF.

#### Activity 1.2.1: Awareness Raising

**Description:** Conduct awareness-raising campaigns to inform the public about climate change impacts, adaptation measures, and mitigation strategies.

**Sub-activities:**

- 1.2.1.1: Development of awareness materials.
- 1.2.1.2: Organization of community events and forums.
- 1.2.1.3: Utilization of media for outreach.

**Deliverables:**

- 1.2.1.1: Awareness materials developed and distributed to 500 households.
- 1.2.1.2: 10 community events conducted, reaching 2,000 individuals.
- 1.2.1.3: Media coverage secured in 3 local newspapers.

#### Activity 1.2.2: Community-Based Adaptation

**Description:** Implement community-based adaptation projects to enhance climate resilience and reduce vulnerability.

**Sub-activities:**

- 1.2.2.1: Identification of vulnerable communities.
- 1.2.2.2: Development of project plans.
- 1.2.2.3: Implementation of adaptation measures.

**Deliverables:**

- 1.2.2.1: 50 vulnerable communities identified and assessed.
- 1.2.2.2: 10 community-based adaptation projects planned.
- 1.2.2.3: 5 adaptation measures implemented, benefiting 1,000 households.

### Notes

- All activities will be monitored and evaluated to ensure compliance with the GCF's fiduciary standards and environmental and social safeguards.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V1.0 PAGE 1/3

## Objective

The objective of this proposal is to enhance access to climate finance for developing countries by leveraging the Green Climate Fund (GCF) and other relevant funding mechanisms.

## Activities

### 2.2.3.1 Enhance GCF Readiness Preparation Proposal (R-PP)

- **Activity:** Develop a comprehensive R-PP that aligns with national priorities and enhances country ownership.
- **Output:** A robust R-PP that meets GCF requirements and is approved by the GCF Board.

### 2.2.3.2 Strengthen National Designated Authorities (NDAs) and National Implementing Entities (NIEs)

- **Activity:** Build capacity of NDAs and NIEs to effectively manage GCF funds.
- **Output:** Enhanced capacities of NDAs and NIEs in accessing, managing, and reporting on GCF funds.

### 2.2.3.3 Develop Project Pipelines

- **Activity:** Identify and develop a pipeline of projects that are aligned with national climate strategies.
- **Output:** A project pipeline ready for submission to the GCF.

## Implementation Strategy

The activities will be implemented through a combination of training, capacity building, and technical assistance. This approach will ensure that developing countries have the necessary skills and knowledge to effectively access and manage GCF funds.

### 2.3.1 Technical Assistance

- **Activity:** Provide targeted technical assistance to NDAs and NIEs.
- **Output:** Enhanced capacities in accessing, managing, and reporting on GCF funds.

### 2.3.2 Capacity Building

- **Activity:** Conduct training workshops and seminars for NDAs and NIEs.
- **Output:** Improved skills and knowledge in climate finance management.

### 2.3.3 Knowledge Management

- **Activity:** Develop and disseminate knowledge products on GCF processes and requirements.
- **Output:** Increased awareness and understanding of GCF procedures among stakeholders.

## Monitoring and Evaluation

The activities will include monitoring and evaluation to ensure effectiveness and accountability. This will involve regular reporting, performance tracking, and feedback mechanisms.

### 2.4.1 Results Framework

- **Activity:** Develop a results framework that outlines expected outcomes and indicators.
- **Output:** A comprehensive results framework aligned with GCF requirements.

### 2.4.2 Monitoring and Evaluation System

- **Activity:** Establish a monitoring and evaluation system to track progress and performance.
- **Output:** An operational M&E system for tracking and reporting on project implementation.

## Financial Resources

The proposal will be funded through a combination of core funding, technical assistance grants, and co-financing from other climate finance sources. The financial resources will ensure sustainability and long-term impact.

### 2.5.1 Core Funding

- **Activity:** Secure core funding to support the overall program.
- **Output:** Stable and sustainable financing for the implementation of activities.

### 2.5.2 Technical Assistance Grants

- **Activity:** Access technical assistance grants from various donors.
- **Output:** Additional resources for capacity building and technical support.

### 2.5.3 Co-financing

- **Activity:** Leverage co-financing opportunities to enhance impact.
- **Output:** Increased funding through partnerships and collaboration.

## Conclusion

This proposal outlines a comprehensive strategy to enhance access to climate finance for developing countries, focusing on capacity building, project development, and effective management of GCF funds. Through targeted activities and strategic implementation, this initiative aims to support developing countries in their efforts to mitigate and adapt to climate change.

## The objective of this proposal is to support the implementation of the National Adaptation Plan (NAP) process in [COUNTRY] by providing technical assistance, vulnerability assessments, and capacity building for climate change adaptation.

### NAP Process
The NAP process aims to identify the priority needs and actions required to adapt to climate change. The NAP process is guided by the following principles:
- **Inclusive**: The NAP process should be inclusive of all stakeholders.
- **Participatory**: The NAP process should be participatory, involving all relevant actors.
- **Gender-responsive**: The NAP process should be gender-responsive and consider the needs of vulnerable groups.
- **Country-driven**: The NAP process should be country-driven and tailored to the specific context of [COUNTRY].
- **Iterative**: The NAP process is iterative and involves continuous learning and adaptation.

### Proposed Activities
The proposed activities for this project include:
1. **Vulnerability Assessments**
   - Conduct vulnerability assessments at national, regional, and local levels.
2. **Capacity Building**
   - Provide training and capacity building for government officials and other stakeholders.
3. **Policy Development**
   - Support the development of climate change adaptation policies and strategies.
4. **Stakeholder Engagement**
   - Facilitate stakeholder engagement and consultations to ensure the NAP process is inclusive and participatory.

### Outputs
The outputs of this project will include:
- **Vulnerability Assessments**: Completed vulnerability assessments at national, regional, and local levels.
- **Capacity Building**: Trained government officials and other stakeholders in climate change adaptation.
- **Policy Development**: Developed climate change adaptation policies and strategies.
- **Stakeholder Engagement**: Facilitated stakeholder engagement and consultations.

### Sub-activities
#### Output 2.1: Baseline data collection and analysis
- Collect baseline data on climate change impacts, vulnerabilities, and adaptive capacities.
- Analyze the collected data to identify priority areas for adaptation.

#### Output 2.2: Development of NAP Roadmap
- Develop a roadmap for the implementation of the NAP process in [COUNTRY].
- Identify key stakeholders and their roles in the NAP process.

### Results Framework
The results framework for this project includes the following indicators:
- **Indicator 1**: Percentage of government officials trained on climate change adaptation.
- **Indicator 2**: Number of vulnerability assessments completed.
- **Indicator 3**: Number of policies and strategies developed.
- **Indicator 4**: Number of stakeholder consultations conducted.

### Budget
The budget for this project is [AMOUNT] USD, which includes:
- **Personnel Costs**: [AMOUNT] USD
- **Travel Costs**: [AMOUNT] USD
- **Equipment Costs**: [AMOUNT] USD
- **Consultancy Fees**: [AMOUNT] USD

### Implementation Plan
The implementation plan for this project is as follows:
1. **Phase 1: Planning and Preparation**
   - Conduct a comprehensive needs assessment.
   - Develop a detailed implementation plan.
2. **Phase 2: Vulnerability Assessments**
   - Conduct vulnerability assessments at national, regional, and local levels.
3. **Phase 3: Capacity Building**
   - Provide training and capacity building for government officials and other stakeholders.
4. **Phase 4: Policy Development**
   - Support the development of climate change adaptation policies and strategies.
5. **Phase 5: Stakeholder Engagement**
   - Facilitate stakeholder engagement and consultations to ensure the NAP process is inclusive and participatory.

### Monitoring and Evaluation
The monitoring and evaluation plan for this project includes:

### Objective

### Strengthening the Great Green Wall Initiatives at country and regional level

#### Output 3: GCF Land restoration, monitoring system at national and regional level upgraded and operational

| Activities | Description | Sub-activities | Outcomes |
|------------|-------------|----------------|----------|
| **Activity 3.1.1: Development of a National Land Restoration Strategy** | The strategy will provide the basis for the GCF land restoration program by identifying priority areas, interventions, and actions to be undertaken. The strategy will also identify key stakeholders and their roles in the implementation process. | - Analysis of existing national policies and strategies<br>- Identification and analysis of priority areas for land restoration<br>- Development of a comprehensive plan for land restoration<br>- Engagement with key stakeholders and development of partnerships<br>- Development of a monitoring system to track progress and impact | - National Land Restoration Strategy developed<br>- Priority areas identified and mapped<br>- Comprehensive plan for land restoration developed<br>- Key stakeholders engaged and partnerships established<br>- Monitoring system in place |
| **Activity 3.1.2: Establishment of a National Land Restoration Trust Fund** | The trust fund will provide financial resources to support the implementation of the national land restoration strategy. It will also facilitate access to additional funding from international sources. | - Development of a business plan for the trust fund<br>- Identification of potential donors and partners<br>- Establishment of the trust fund<br>- Development of guidelines for the use of funds<br>- Monitoring and evaluation of the trust fund's performance | - National Land Restoration Trust Fund established<br>- Business plan developed<br>- Potential donors and partners identified<br>- Guidelines for fund usage developed<br>- Monitoring and evaluation system in place |
| **Activity 3.1.2: Establishment of a Regional Land Restoration Trust Fund** | The regional trust fund will provide financial resources to support the implementation of regional land restoration initiatives. It will also facilitate access to additional funding from international sources. | - Development of a business plan for the regional trust fund<br>- Identification of potential donors and partners<br>- Establishment of the regional trust fund<br>- Development of guidelines for the use of funds<br>- Monitoring and evaluation of the trust fund's performance | - Regional Land Restoration Trust Fund established<br>- Business plan developed<br>- Potential donors and partners identified<br>- Guidelines for fund usage developed<br>- Monitoring and evaluation system in place |

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V1.0 PAGE 117

## Activity 3.1.5 Development of the Integrated Monitoring and Evaluation System (IMES)

### 3.1.5.1 Objective
To develop a robust, comprehensive and user-friendly IMES that will support the monitoring and evaluation of the GCF-funded projects.

### 3.1.5.2 Description of Work
The development of the IMES will be carried out in three phases:
- Phase 1: Design and Planning
- Phase 2: Development and Testing
- Phase 3: Implementation and Evaluation

### 3.1.5.3 Deliverables
- Detailed design document for the IMES
- Functional specifications for the IMES
- Developed and tested IMES system
- User manuals and training materials for the IMES
- Evaluation report on the effectiveness of the IMES

## Activity 3.1.6 Building the Capacity of Recipient Countries to Monitor and Evaluate GCF-Funded Projects

### 3.1.6.1 Objective
To build the capacity of recipient countries to monitor and evaluate GCF-funded projects effectively.

### 3.1.6.2 Description of Work
The activities will include:
- Conducting needs assessments
- Developing training materials
- Delivering training workshops
- Providing technical assistance

### 3.1.6.3 Deliverables
- Capacity assessment reports for recipient countries
- Training materials and manuals
- Training workshop reports
- Technical assistance reports

## Activity 3.1.7 Preparation of the Biennial Report on the Implementation of GCF-Funded Projects

### 3.1.7.1 Objective
To prepare a biennial report that provides an overview of the implementation status and progress of GCF-funded projects.

### 3.1.7.2 Description of Work
The activities will include:
- Collecting data from recipient countries
- Analyzing the data
- Preparing the report

### 3.1.7.3 Deliverables
- Biennial report on the implementation of GCF-funded projects
- Executive summary of the biennial report

## Activity 3.1.8 Development of a Framework for Monitoring, Reporting and Verification (MRV) of Greenhouse Gas Emissions

### 3.1.8.1 Objective
To develop a framework that will enable recipient countries to monitor, report and verify their greenhouse gas emissions.

### 3.1.8.2 Description of Work
The activities will include:
- Conducting a literature review
- Developing the MRV framework
- Piloting the framework in selected countries

### 3.1.8.3 Deliverables
- Framework for MRV of greenhouse gas emissions
- Pilot test reports from selected countries

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V1.0: PAGE 118

## Objective
Support national governments to enhance their capacity to access and effectively use climate finance from sources such as the Green Climate Fund (GCF) and other international climate funds.

### Applicability 1.1.1
Develop the necessary tools, guidelines and procedures for accessing GCF resources.
- **Guideline 1.1.1.1**: Develop a comprehensive guide on GCF access processes.
- **Guideline 1.1.1.2**: Provide training programs to build capacity in GCF-related activities.

### Applicability 1.1.2
Establish National Designated Authorities (NDAs) and Accredited Entities (AEs).
- **Action 1.1.2.1**: Establish NDAs with clear mandates and responsibilities.
- **Action 1.1.2.2**: Accredit entities to access GCF funds.

### Applicability 1.1.3
Develop a pipeline of projects that are ready for GCF funding.
- **Action 1.1.3.1**: Identify potential projects and conduct feasibility studies.
- **Action 1.1.3.2**: Prepare project proposals in alignment with GCF requirements.

### Applicability 1.1.4
Strengthen the capacity of national and regional institutions to manage climate finance.
- **Action 1.1.4.1**: Develop training programs for financial management.
- **Action 1.1.4.2**: Establish monitoring and evaluation systems for climate finance projects.

### Applicability 1.1.5
Promote public-private partnerships (PPPs) to leverage private sector investment in climate projects.
- **Action 1.1.5.1**: Facilitate dialogue between government and private sector entities.
- **Action 1.1.5.2**: Develop guidelines for PPPs in the context of GCF-funded projects.

### Applicability 1.1.6
Enhance transparency and accountability in climate finance management.
- **Action 1.1.6.1**: Implement robust reporting systems.
- **Action 1.1.6.2**: Conduct regular audits to ensure compliance with GCF standards.

## Objective 3.1 National and Regional GCF Implementation Planning and Coordination

### Applicability 3.1.1
Develop a comprehensive national implementation plan for accessing and utilizing GCF resources.
- **Action 3.1.1.1**: Conduct a needs assessment to identify priority areas for GCF funding.
- **Action 3.1.1.2**: Develop a strategic plan outlining the steps for GCF engagement.

### Applicability 3.1.2
Establish a national coordinating mechanism (NCM) to oversee GCF-related activities.
- **Action 3.1.2.1**: Define the roles and responsibilities of the NCM.
- **Action 3.1.2.2**: Ensure effective coordination among relevant stakeholders.

### Applicability 3.1.3
Strengthen the capacity of national institutions to manage GCF-funded projects.
- **Action 3.1.3.1**: Provide training programs on project management and financial oversight.
- **Action 3.1.3.2**: Establish a system for monitoring and evaluating project progress.

### Applicability 3.1.4
Promote regional cooperation to enhance the impact of GCF-funded projects.
- **Action 3.1.4.1**: Facilitate regional dialogues on climate finance.
- **Action 3.1.4.2**: Develop joint proposals for regional projects.

### Applicability 3.1.5
Enhance transparency and accountability in the management of GCF resources.
- **Action 3.1.5.1**: Implement robust reporting systems to track resource allocation.
- **Action 3.1.5.2**: Conduct regular audits to ensure compliance with GCF standards.

## Objective 4.1 Public Awareness and Engagement

### Applicability 4.1.1
Raise public awareness about the importance of climate finance for sustainable development.
- **Action 4.1.1.1**: Develop communication strategies to engage the public.
- **Action 4.1.1.2**: Conduct public outreach campaigns on GCF-funded projects.

### Applicability 4.1.2
Encourage active participation of civil society organizations in climate finance activities.
- **Action 4.1.2.1**: Establish partnerships with CSOs to enhance engagement.
- **Action 4.1.2.2**: Provide platforms for CSOs to contribute to policy dialogues.

### Applicability 4.1.3
Promote transparency and accountability in climate finance initiatives.
- **Action 4.1.3.1**: Implement open data policies to increase accessibility of information.
- **Action 4.1.3.2**: Conduct public consultations to gather feedback on climate finance activities.

### Applicability 4.1.4
Strengthen the capacity of media outlets to report on climate finance issues.
- **Action 4.1.4.1**: Provide training programs for journalists on climate finance.
- **Action 4.1.4.2**: Establish a network of media partners to amplify climate finance stories.

### Applicability 4.1.5
Encourage private sector engagement in climate finance initiatives.
- **Action 4.1.5.1**: Facilitate dialogues between the public and private sectors on climate finance.
- **Action 4.1.5.2**: Develop incentives for private sector participation in GCF-funded projects.

## Objective

Support English-speaking developing countries to access and effectively implement climate change mitigation and adaptation projects.

### Objective 3.2: Narrow and prevent climate change environmental gaps and opportunities through capacity building in developing countries.

| Activities | Description | Sub-activities | Benefits |
|------------|-------------|----------------|----------|
| **Activity 3.2.1: Strengthen capacity to access and effectively implement low-carbon development projects** | Enhance the capacity of developing countries to access, prepare, and negotiate through and effectively implement climate change mitigation and adaptation projects. | 3.2.1.1: Develop National Designated Authorities (NDAs) and National Implementing Entities (NIEs). <br> 3.2.1.2: Provide technical assistance to NDAs and NIEs. <br> 3.2.1.3: Build capacity of project proponents in developing countries. | 3.2.1.1: Enhanced capacity of NDAs and NIEs to access and implement projects. <br> 3.2.1.2: Improved technical assistance for NDAs and NIEs. <br> 3.2.1.3: Increased number of project proposals submitted by developing countries. |
| **Activity 3.2.2: Fill information gaps** | Address information gaps that hinder the effective implementation of climate change mitigation and adaptation projects in developing countries. | 3.2.2.1: Develop a knowledge management system to capture, store, and share lessons learned from project implementation. <br> 3.2.2.2: Conduct regional workshops to disseminate best practices and lessons learned. <br> 3.2.2.3: Publish a series of knowledge products (e.g., case studies, good practice notes). | 3.2.2.1: Improved access to information and knowledge for project proponents in developing countries. <br> 3.2.2.2: Enhanced dissemination of best practices and lessons learned. <br> 3.2.2.3: Increased awareness and understanding of effective implementation strategies among stakeholders. |

The activity will ensure that the capacity-building efforts are tailored to the needs of developing countries, thereby enhancing their ability to access and effectively implement climate change mitigation and adaptation projects.

## SURAGOWA and Local Adaptation Actions Post-projecting

The proposed project aims to develop a comprehensive monitoring system for the SURAGOWA program, which is implemented in several provinces in Indonesia. This system will ensure the sustainability of the action plan and provide a basis for future planning. The project will also establish a comprehensive and robust monitoring system, assess the qualitative impact of the actions taken, and identify areas that need further improvement.

### SURAGOWA Biennial Outcome Reporting

Independent consultants or research institutions will be engaged to conduct an evaluation of the SURAGOWA program's implementation and identify outcomes in relation to objectives, outputs, and activities. The evaluation will also assess the effectiveness of the monitoring system in providing reliable data for decision-making. The biennial outcome report will include recommendations on how to improve the program's performance and provide suggestions for future actions.

The SURAGOWA program aims to support local governments in developing and implementing climate change adaptation plans, as well as to strengthen their capacity to access climate finance. The program also seeks to enhance the resilience of vulnerable communities and ecosystems by promoting sustainable development practices.

# RISK ASSESSMENT AND MANAGEMENT

## Risk Register

### Section Risk Factor 1: Displacement & Insecurity, social instability

| Category       | Probability | Impact   |
|----------------|-------------|----------|
| **Description**| **Medium**  | **High** |

230. The use of climate change as a pretext to justify violence and human rights abuses is a potential risk of violence in the Sahel region. Climate change exacerbates existing social, economic, and political tensions that are already high due to poor governance and weak institutions. These tensions can lead to conflict in the region, particularly among pastoralists and farmers, competing for access to land and water resources. The impact on communities is exacerbated by weak governance structures, lack of rule of law, and inadequate security forces. The government security strategy, comprised of "Baraza" (local councils) and community policing initiatives, is insufficient to address the root causes of conflict. This situation is also complicated by regional instability and ongoing SURGOMIRA conflicts.

231. A lasting peace in the Sahel region requires SURGOMIRA to build local capacity where there is limited trust and disagreement is high. The government needs to identify leaders and initiatives that are able to work with communities to address these issues. This includes addressing the root causes of conflict, such as weak institutions, lack of rule of law, and poor governance.

### Section Risk Factor 2: Corruption and mismanagement

| Category       | Probability | Impact   |
|----------------|-------------|----------|
| **Description**| **Medium**  |

240. Corruption in the Sahel is generally ranked as a major risk factor and governance weakness. Governance weaknesses include corruption, lack of transparency, and weak rule of law. These factors undermine development efforts and hinder economic growth. Corruption undermines public sector performance, reduces investment, and discourages foreign direct investment. It also contributes to poverty and inequality by diverting resources away from the intended beneficiaries.

241. Corruption in the Sahel is exacerbated by weak institutions, lack of transparency, and a culture of impunity. The government has taken steps to address corruption, including establishing anti-corruption agencies and implementing international standards. However, these efforts have been hindered by political will and lack of resources.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND (FUNDING) PROPOSAL V.1.0 - PAGE 13

## Section: Risk Factor #8: Anti-Money Laundering and Countering Financial Terrorism Risk

### Project Title:
Green Climate Fund (GCF)

### Sponsor:
Ministry of Finance

### Objective:
To ensure the implementation of anti-money laundering and countering financial terrorism measures in the project.

### Risks:
- **Risks of money laundering and countering the financing of terrorism:** The overall risk is assessed as high, but the likelihood is low. The Government has implemented a comprehensive AML/CFT framework.
- **Compliance with international standards:** The Government shall comply and shall require all persons and entities engaged in its activities under the project to comply with the provisions of the AML/CFT Act 2008 and any other relevant legislation.
- **Government's commitment:** The Government commits to ensure that sufficient undertakings from all persons and entities involved in its activities are obtained. This shall be consistent with the substantive requirements of the GCF’s Policy on Prohibited Practices.
- **GCF's role:** The GCF will only provide funding if the Government has implemented adequate measures to prevent, detect, and report money laundering and terrorist financing. The Government and GCF are jointly responsible for ensuring that these measures are in place.
- **Due diligence:** The Government is required to provide details of its anti-money laundering and countering financial terrorism framework to the GCF. This includes the identification of risks, the establishment of a risk-based approach, and the implementation of appropriate measures.
- **GCF's oversight:** The GCF will establish appropriate monitoring and control measures to ensure that materials, technology, and services are used for their intended purposes. This includes ongoing due diligence and compliance with relevant laws and regulations.
- **Government's responsibility:** The Government is responsible for ensuring that all entities involved in the project comply with anti-money laundering and countering financial terrorism requirements.

### Mitigation:
All risks will be managed through a robust risk management framework, including regular monitoring and reporting. The GCF and the Government will work together to ensure the effectiveness of these measures.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUND (FUNDING) PROPOSAL V.3.0 - PAGE 13

## 267

Within the Sahel, small-scale farmers make up approximately 80% of the agricultural labor force and play a critical role in feeding local communities. They also face high levels of poverty and food insecurity. The African Development Bank's (AfDB) Desert to Power initiative aims to address these challenges by promoting sustainable development through renewable energy. This initiative focuses on solar power generation, which can lead to economic growth, job creation, and improved living standards for rural communities. By providing access to clean energy, the AfDB seeks to enhance food security, reduce poverty, and promote sustainable development.

## 268

Gender choice strategies and decision-making often favor women, as they are primarily responsible for collecting water and fuel, and caring for local animals. Climate variability and extreme weather events exacerbate these challenges by increasing the time required to collect water and fuel. Women's vulnerability to climate change is compounded by their limited access to education, health care, and economic opportunities. Addressing gender inequalities in climate adaptation strategies is essential to ensure that women have equal access to resources and decision-making processes.

## 269

The Sahel region faces significant challenges related to food security and agricultural productivity. Despite these obstacles, there are numerous success stories of farmers who have adapted to the harsh conditions and improved their yields through innovative practices. For example, in Niger, farmers have adopted techniques such as zero-tillage, crop rotation, and agroforestry to enhance soil fertility and water retention. These practices not only increase crop yields but also contribute to long-term sustainability by preserving natural resources.

## 270

SURAGGONA is aligned with the GGC Gender Policy, which recognizes that gender equality, rights, and empowerment are essential for sustainable development. The policy emphasizes the importance of integrating gender considerations into all aspects of project design, implementation, and monitoring. An analysis of the gender issues and recommendations in the Sahel region highlights the need to address the unique challenges faced by women and men in rural communities. By promoting equal access to resources and opportunities, SURAGGONA aims to empower both women and men to participate fully in decision-making processes and benefit from the project's outcomes.

## 272
Financial management and procurement under this programme will be guided by relevant FAO rules and regulations based on the Agreement between FAO and UNDP. UNDP will be responsible for sub-contracting and procurement, including the use of UNDP’s procurement system. FAO will provide technical support to the NAPA focal points and other relevant stakeholders in preparing proposals and managing projects. ## 273
FAO has developed an Online Budget resource planning system termed the Global Resource Management System (GRMS). This system is used by all FAO country offices and headquarters. The system has improved the flow of financial information and supports financial monitoring and reporting. It also provides a platform for the preparation of budgets and annual programme budgets. The system is based on a web-based platform that allows for real-time access to data, management and more efficient reporting.

### G.4. Disclosure of funding proposal

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.0 - PAGE 13

## ANNEXES

### H.1. Mandatory annexes

- **Annex 1**: NDA non-disclosure agreement (mandatory)
- **Annex 2**: Funding study and a detailed study application
- **Annex 3**: Detailed budget (mandatory)
- **Annex 4**: Detailed study plan (mandatory)
- **Annex 5**: Implementation timeline including the programme calendar (mandatory)
- **Annex 6**: E&I document corresponding to the E&I category (A, B or C or if 1, 2 or 3)
  - Environmental and Social Impact Assessment (ESIA) or
  - Environmental and Social Management Plan (ESMP) or
  - Environmental and Social Management System (ESMS)
- **Annex 7**: Other relevant studies, e.g. Resettlement Action Plan, Resettlement Policy Framework, Gender Action Plan, etc.
- **Annex 8**: Summary of institutions and subordinate programme plan
- **Annex 9**: Gender assessment and project/programme-level action plan (mandatory)
- **Annex 10**: Long-term climate change risks and insurance
- **Annex 11**: Financial risk management plan (mandatory)
- **Annex 12**: AEI fee request (mandatory)
- **Annex 13**: Co-financing commitment letter, application (mandatory)
- **Annex 14**: Team visit including a detailed visit schedule and if applicable, resettlement schedule
- **Annex 15**: Other annexes as applicable

### H.2. Optional annexes

- **Annex 16**: Multi-country projects information (mandatory)
- **Annex 17**: Multi-country project/programme information (mandatory)
- **Annex 18**: Project/programme information on the impact of climate change on the costing of financing and/or scaling up of projects
- **Annex 19**: Project/programme information by focal points on existing national/regional projects
- **Annex 20**: First level AMC/CPT/NCV assessment
- **Annex 21**: Assessment of GHG emission reductions and their monitoring and reporting (mandatory)
- **Annex 22**: Evidence of the existence of a national climate change policy

*Please note that a binding proposal will be considered complete only when all of the applicable supporting documents are included.*

## Dear Honourable Sir,

I am writing to you on behalf of the Minister of Livestock, Agriculture, and Fisheries (MADP) and Director General of the National Fund for Food and Agriculture (FNA), to inform you that the funding proposal submitted by Food and Agriculture Organization to its Office in Djibouti has been approved.

In this regard, I am pleased to forward herewith the letter from the authorized representative of H. Doc Debeleh Denon, the National Director General of OGD, regarding the approval of the project.

Following the decision 0.0/22, the content of which we attach herewith for your reference, we hereby confirm that the funding proposal has been approved and that the funds will be released accordingly.

By confirming our acceptance, it is implied that:

1. The Government of Djibouti will incorporate the project into its national budget.
2. The project includes the funding proposal in conformity with the technical norms.
3. It is aligned with the GGP's intervention areas and social objectives; the project as included in the funding proposal is consistent with the objectives of the GGP.
4. The state budget will cover all administrative expenses to the project as included in the funding proposal that were not covered by the funds.

### et Président du Conseil d'Administration
### du Fonds Vert National du Mali (FVNM)
### à
### Le Green Climate Fund (GCF)

### Objet: Financing proposal for the GCF by Food and Security Organization regarding scaling up Resilience in Africa's Green Wall (RAAGW)

### Monsieur, Sir,

Nous avons le plaisir d'adresser à votre aimable attention la proposition de financement pour le Fonds Vert Climatique (FVC) par l'Organisation des Nations Unies pour l'Alimentation et l'Agriculture (FAO) concernant l'extension du Projet de Résilience en Afrique Verte (RAAGW) au Mali.

Le projet vise à renforcer les capacités institutionnelles et la résilience des communautés locales dans le Sahel, en particulier celles qui sont vulnérables aux changements climatiques. Le projet sera mis en œuvre par la FAO avec un financement de 10 millions USD sur une période de cinq ans (2023-2028).

Dans ce cadre, nous vous prions d'agréer, Monsieur le Directeur Général, l'expression de notre haute considération.

# Secretariat’s assessment of FP268

## Proposed name:
Scaling-Up Benefits to Africa’s Great Green Wall (SB4A/GGW)

## Accredited entity:
Forest and Agriculture Organization of the United Nations (FAO)

## Country(ies):
Benin, Burkina Faso, Chad, Ethiopia, Mali, Mauritania, Niger, Nigeria, Senegal

## Proposed/grant size:
Medium

## I. Overall assessment of the Secretariat

### Strengths
The programme directly supports the GEF-7 strategic goal and objective to enhance benefits from ecosystem services and sustainable management of natural resources by restoring 10 million hectares of degraded land and sequestering 25 million tons of carbon dioxide equivalent (CO2e) by 2030 through large-scale land restoration. The programme aims to foster a paradigm shift in the way land degradation is addressed, focusing on integrated landscape approaches that address land degradation and climate change simultaneously. It leverages existing regional and national initiatives such as the Great Green Wall (GGW) initiative, the Pan-African Vision for Ecological Restoration (PAVIR), and the Great Green Wall Accelerator (GGWA). The programme also aims to promote sustainable land management practices that enhance ecosystem resilience, strengthen food security and livelihoods, and support climate change mitigation and adaptation. The programme is aligned with the GEF-7 strategic goal of enhancing benefits from ecosystem services and sustainable management of natural resources.

### Points of concern
The programme is to be implemented over a period of 10 years (2023–2032), which is longer than the standard GEF project cycle of six years. This may pose challenges in terms of maintaining focus, accountability, and sustainability over such a long period. To address these concerns, the programme should include robust monitoring and evaluation systems, including a clear plan for adaptive management measures, to ensure that it remains on track towards achieving its objectives.

The Board may wish to consider approving this funding proposal in accordance with the terms set out above. The Secretariat will provide additional information on the conditions set out in paragraph 10 of document GEF/42/02.

## II. Summary of the Secretariat’s assessment

### Z.1. Programme background


---
**Page 159**
---

# The problem statement within the GGR region requires significant climate-related vulnerabilities and developmental deficits. A substantial segment of their populations resides in rural areas, where livelihoods are predominantly reliant on agriculture, forestry, and fisheries, with most rural communities having limited access to basic infrastructure such as electricity, clean water, sanitation, and health services. These communities have faced prolonged droughts, increased rainfall and extreme degradation but have shown resilience through traditional knowledge systems.

## 1. Scaling-Up Resilience in Africa's Great Green Wall (SUNGGW)

The SUNGGW program is a regional initiative that aims to enhance the resilience of vulnerable communities across eight Sahelian countries: Burkina Faso, Chad, Djibouti, Mali, Mauritania, Niger, Nigeria and Senegal.

### The program

The program has three main objectives:

- **Objective 1:** Enhance the resilience of vulnerable communities through improved land management practices, sustainable natural resource use, climate-smart agriculture, and water management.
- **Objective 2:** Strengthen the capacity of national institutions to implement and monitor climate change adaptation and mitigation strategies.
- **Objective 3:** Promote regional cooperation and knowledge sharing among countries.

The program is funded by the Green Climate Fund (GCF) with a total budget of USD 219,875,000. The GCF contribution to the GGR program is USD 150,156,438. The remaining funds are provided by the governments of the United Nations (UN), the Government of Burkina Faso, Chad, Ethiopia, Mali, Mauritania, Niger, Nigeria and Senegal.

### Program targets

The program targets approximately 1.8 million direct beneficiaries and 4.6 million indirect beneficiaries across the eight countries. The region is characterized by a semi-arid climate with an average annual rainfall of less than 500 mm.

## 2. Component-by-component analysis

### Component 1: Landscape, land-use, conservation in agricultural, climatic change, ecosystem services and biodiversity management

This component will address the issues of land degradation by mapping soil types, land use, vegetation cover, and water resources. It aims to promote sustainable land management practices that enhance ecosystem resilience and support livelihoods.

#### Activities include:

- **Activity 1:** Develop a comprehensive database on land-use patterns and changes over time.
- **Activity 2:** Map key ecological features such as wetlands, forests, and grasslands.
- **Activity 3:** Identify priority areas for conservation and restoration.

# Green Climate Fund

## 3.2 Paradigm shift potential

### a.
The program aims to reach approximately 180 million direct beneficiaries and 1.80 billion indirect beneficiaries, essentially contributing to an equitable, gender-sensitive system. Through this approach, the program will address the needs of vulnerable groups such as women, youth, and indigenous peoples. The program will also aim to mitigate and adapt to climate change by integrating gender-responsive programming that will also introduce targeted climate-change-related livelihood options, for example, through sustainable agriculture and renewable energy for low-income households and women.

**Scale:** Medium to high

## 3.3 Sustainable development potential

### a.
The program will contribute to several Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs), specifically SDG 1 (No Poverty) by reducing poverty rates, SDG 5 (Gender Equality) by promoting gender equality, and SDG 7 (Affordable and Clean Energy) through the promotion of renewable energy. The program also aims to contribute to SDG 8 (Decent Work and Economic Growth), SDG 13 (Climate Action), and SDG 15 (Life on Land).

**Scale:** High

## 3.4 Needs of the recipient

### a.
The program will address key development needs for the PROGRAM countries, including high degrees of vulnerability and lack of access to investment, governance, and social infrastructure. The program aims to contribute to climate resilience and sustainable development by addressing the needs of vulnerable groups with a low financing capacity.

**Scale:** High

## 3.5 Country oversight

### a.
No independent bodies have been identified by the Governments of all target countries.

### b.
To improve oversight, the African Union launched the GOW initiative in 2007 to promote good governance and fight corruption. The program will also aim to strengthen national oversight mechanisms for development financing and adaptation programs. This includes promoting transparency and accountability through independent oversight bodies, improving public financial management systems, and establishing robust internal controls.

**Scale:** High

## 3.6 Efficiency and effectiveness

### a.
The program aims to promote good governance by strengthening the capacity of recipient countries to manage climate change-related funds effectively. The program will also aim to improve the efficiency and effectiveness of development assistance through better coordination among donors, improved monitoring and evaluation systems, and enhanced transparency.

**Scale:** Medium to high

# Impact of the intervention period

The programme will also facilitate the development of a robust retail network for agri-food products. In addition, emergency rations, food assistance and cash transfers will be provided to vulnerable households. The programme will also support the government's private sector participation.

The programme is expected to generate 17,500 jobs directly in the agricultural value chain and create an additional 34,800 jobs indirectly through increased demand for goods and services. This will increase household incomes by 25% on average and the earnings of USD 92 per tonne of carbon dioxide equivalent in avoided emissions. The programme is expected to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 1.8 Mt CO2e annually.

As a result, the programme should lead to an environmentally sustainable agri-food system with increased resilience to climate change. The programme will also contribute to the achievement of the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) and the Paris Agreement on Climate Change. The programme is expected to generate significant environmental benefits, including reduced greenhouse gas emissions, improved water quality, and enhanced biodiversity.

The results of the financial analysis show high profitability for beneficiaries and the government. The programme is expected to generate a net present value (NPV) of USD 178 million over the period of 20 years. This will yield a financial internal rate of return (IRR) of 14% per annum over the period of 20 years.

## Assessment of consistency with GCP safeguards and policies

### 4.1 Environmental and social safeguards

The Environmental and Social Management Framework (ESMF) charted by the AF will be used to ensure that the programme is consistent with environmental and social (E&S) safeguard standards. The ESMF was developed in line with the World Bank Group's (WBG) Environmental, Social, Health and Safety (ESHS) General EHS Guidelines for Category B Projects. The ESMF was developed to ensure that the programme has been designed at a category B compliance level with environmental and social risk management standards.

#### ESMF - Animal Husbandry and Agricultural Credit for Smallholder Agriculture

The programme is expected to help establish an environment where the land and water rights of smallholders are protected. The programme will also be implemented in close partnership with local governments, NGOs and other stakeholders. The programme will also provide training and technical assistance to smallholders to improve their productivity.

#### ESMF - Forest and Pastoral Management

The programme is expected to help establish an environment where the land and water rights of pastoralists are protected. The programme will also be implemented in close partnership with local governments, NGOs and other stakeholders. The programme will also provide training and technical assistance to pastoralists to improve their productivity.

Additionally, the programme will provide direct benefits to local communities through increased access to markets and improved livelihoods.
